- 2026-10-20 — **HEROSIM_EXCHANGE_TCP: SCIENTIST's condition (the only other caller of the switched function) and two notes, filed before any cell (S5; descriptive, no label).** The caveat of the entries below, in the wording the expert fixed: **under the switch only the simulated charge changes; neither scheduler's placement cost nor its in-flight work estimate includes the TCP term.**
  - **Callers of `_peer_exchange_time`: two.** `infrastructure.py` (the input stage's own charge, `record=True`, the switched path) and `snapshot_fidelity._expected_input_seconds` (`record=False`, an estimate for the fidelity replay).
  - **The fidelity path is inactive in every cell of this study.**
    - `snapshot_fidelity.capture` runs only when `HEROSIM_SNAPSHOT_FIDELITY=1`. The gate sets that variable only for the oracle arms (`cd_oracle`, `cd_oracle_ib0`, which also set `HEROSIM_PG_ORACLE=1`; the two plan-oracle paths in `peer_greedy_network/scheduler.py` that call `capture_batch_snapshot` are behind it) and for sealed-trigger cells (`R1A_SNAPSHOT_PLAN`, not set here).
    - `r1a_gate.sbatch` unsets every `HEROSIM_*` and `LIVE_AUDIT_*` variable at entry, and the arms of this study (`cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100`, FL gnn `ra_gnn_eng_combfl_nosplit_k100`) set none of them.
    - Checked per cell after the run: no summary's `env` block may carry `HEROSIM_SNAPSHOT_FIDELITY`, `HEROSIM_PG_ORACLE` or a `LIVE_AUDIT_*` variable; any that does is named.
  - **No live scheduler estimate reaches the switched path.**
    - The peer-greedy rule's exchange cost is `_pg_exchange_seconds` (`peer_greedy_network/scheduler.py:514-522`), which calls `_payload_transfer_time` and `peer_link_latency` directly and never `_peer_exchange_time`; `heft_network` does the same and is not an arm here.
    - The in-flight estimate (`live_audit.inflight_full_seconds`, the `cd_ib0_inflightfull` bar) prices a task in its input stage at execution + `_approx_comm` (storage rate, no exchange), and a task past it from its realized `compute_start_time`. The charged exchange moves those start times, which is the physics changing, not the estimate pricing it.
    - The learned arm's features (`reduced_features.py`, `partial_state_v6`) and the GNN autoscaler read the peer table and routes, never the charged term.
  - **Note 1 (validity range).** The model is validated on 4–500 MB only (R0). It is applied to every remote transfer size, including payloads under 4 MB.
  - **Note 2 (smoke and its twin).** The switch-on smoke cell is kept, with its md5, and disclosed. Its twin in the 288-cell grid must reproduce it field for field (R0).

  - **Code, additions only.**

- 2026-10-20 — **TCP-transfer sensitivity (HEROSIM_EXCHANGE_TCP), design and SCIENTIST's prediction, filed before any cell (descriptive, no label; never replaces a filed result; the user may veto any paper use).**
  - **Why.** S5's shaped-link check (netns_result.json md5 9de512ea) found that real TCP on the simulator's own rates and latencies costs MTU framing (×1514/1448) plus a handshake and slow start, and the peer-exchange charge has neither.
  - **Switch (default off, additions-only; B3 byte-identical with it off).** HEROSIM_EXCHANGE_TCP=1 replaces each non-co-located peer transfer's size/bottleneck term in `_peer_exchange_time` with a zero-parameter TCP model. With λ = the route's one-way latency (unchanged and still added once), RTT = 2λ, goodput g = bottleneck × 1448/1514, initcwnd w0 = 10 × 1448 bytes, BDP = g × RTT:
    - time = RTT (handshake) + one RTT per slow-start round while cwnd < BDP and bytes remain (sending cwnd, then doubling it) + the remaining bytes ÷ g.
    - Co-located pairs stay free. Peers stay summed serially, each transfer with its own handshake. Ingress and image pulls are unchanged.
    - Validation, as an acceptance check before any cell: the implemented function reproduces S5's 20 n = 1 medians to within 0.15 s on 18 of them and 0.3 s on all, as SCIENTIST's offline check did (worst −0.29 s at 500 MB, 75 MB/s).
  - **Arms (12 gate topologies 19537–19548 × g0–g3, `R1A_SLATE=declared_pruning_v1_uncapped`, configs exactly `comb/in_comball`, switch on).** Bar `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` s0 moderate and heavy (48 + 48); FL gnn `ra_gnn_eng_combfl_nosplit_k100` s1–2 moderate and heavy (96 + 96); 288 cells.
    - The switch-off comparison is S4's 0.7 arms. They must match the new pin through an identity rerun (bar and FL gnn s1, switch off, field for field) and a smoke cell with the switch on, filed first.
    - Every learned cell asserts its checkpoint path and md5: s1 a670eb0c67577bcb63785f514bb63534, s2 78c09cc0daf315587dcf6c9c5c18aa28 (comb/md5_comball.json).
  - **Rows.** Paired over topologies, Wilcoxon unadjusted.
    - R0: identity, smoke and the validation check.
    - R1: paired FL-vs-bar median % per rung × {whole, after-360}, switch on vs off.
    - R2: the bar's heavy − moderate decomposition (load_anomaly terms) on vs off.
    - R3: per arm × rung: exchange s per task, transfers per task, lock wait, mean replicas.
  - **Prediction (SCIENTIST, 2026-10-20; blind to every switch-on cell).** The open-loop arithmetic (filed means, load_anomaly.txt) adds about 0.8 s per task to the bar and 0.9 s to FL gnn at moderate, and 0.5 vs 0.6 s at heavy. FL gnn makes more transfers (1.08 vs 0.99 per task at moderate) on faster links, and a fixed per-transfer cost is larger on faster links.
    - R1, moderate whole: the FL-vs-bar median moves toward the bar by 3–6 pp. Its sign is not predicted.
    - R1, heavy whole: it moves toward the bar by 2–5 pp.
    - R1, after-360: both rungs move in the same direction; the size is not predicted.
    - R2: the bar's heavy − moderate gap widens (more negative) by 0.15–0.45 s whole; moderate has more transfers per task.
    - R3: exchange per task rises for every arm at both rungs. Lock wait and mean replicas rise, because exchange time holds the replica.
    - Closed-loop effects may amplify the open-loop part. The prediction is for direction and range, not exact values.
  - **Reading rules, fixed now.**
    - (a) If R1 moderate whole under the switch is above −2 % (the program's win line), the paper states in the abstract that the size of the gain depends on the transfer model, with the number.
    - (b) If its sign flips, no headline magnitude stands without the user's decision.
    - (c) If R1 moves by less than 1.5 pp on every test, the paper's TCP sentence gains that number and nothing else changes.

Panic ticks rise 7–13 % (12/12 in every arm × rung). Mean replicas stay flat: R3 of SCIENTIST's prediction FAILED. Reading rule (c) fires: the heavy after-360 FL-vs-bar median moves +1.88 pp. Verdict per row (SCIENTIST, accepted as filed, 18cc1cc5 verified): R0 deterministic; R4 held; R3 FAILED; R1 held; R2 signs kept, rule (c) fires.

    - Checks that held in every cell: switch state, clean pin `90b6afe5`, and, in every learned cell, the checkpoint md5 against `comb/md5_comball.json` (s1 `a670eb0c`, s2 `78c09cc0`).

    - Per rung, on − off: moderate −0.060 s whole (p .0005) and −0.069 s after-360 (p .0005); heavy −0.020 s (p .092) and −0.024 s (p .077). So with the switch on the bar is faster, mostly at moderate.

- 2026-10-20 — **Simulator component check: TCP over shaped links, descriptive, no label (S5; SCIENTIST's criteria and fit; the expert's task).** Real TCP over a tc-shaped veth pair against the pipelined charge the cells run. It checks that TCP over a shaped link behaves like `size / bottleneck + route latency` and shows how concurrent flows compare with the simulator's two sharing rules. It does not show that real cellular or Wi-Fi links have these rates: the rates are the simulator's own (`ACCESS_CLASSES`). Store-and-forward is not tested; no uncut_groups cell uses it.
  - **Setup.** Harness `scripts_cosim/validation/netns_transfer_check.py` (md5 `b6538092c8ce0d5d526399feea5bc969`; branch `s5/netns-check`, run at `0a87adde`, since rebased onto reference-physics `b25296f7` with the file unchanged). Two network namespaces joined by one veth pair created inside them; netem one-way delay on both ends (RTT = 2 × latency); tbf at the pair's bottleneck on the sender end; tc and sysctl only inside the namespaces; cubic, TCP buffer caps 64 MiB. The charge is computed in-process from `cc40s19537.json` (md5 `24c615178d675fef06f844de8bd5cae3`, read from datalab) with `NetworkFabric.hops` / `route_latency`, never from a table. Five real server pairs, none dropped: wired_wired node21>node18 (3 hops, 117 MB/s, 51.5 ms); cellular_to_wired node1>node14 (6 hops, 4.0 MB/s, 54.2 ms); wired_to_cellular node14>node1 (6 hops, 75.0 MB/s, 54.2 ms); wifi_low node5>node3 (6 hops, 7.21 MB/s, 62.9 ms); wifi_high node2>node3 (4 hops, 13.48 MB/s, 55.9 ms). Grid: n = 1 at 4/50/250/500 MB; n = 2 and 4 at 50 and 250 MB; 3 repetitions; 120 runs, all payload digests OK. Output JSON md5 `9de512ea1f91f292edc86f56420fb0b6`.
  - **Attachments.** `uncut_groups_s0_v1/netns_check/`: `netns_result.json`, `table.txt`, `netns_transfer_check.py.copy`.
  - **Fit of the n = 1 medians, measured = a × charge + b over 50/250/500 MB (SCIENTIST's refit).**

    | Case (bottleneck) | a | b (s) |
    |---|---|---|
    | cellular_to_wired (4 MB/s) | 1.0460 | 0.52 |
    | wifi_low (7.2) | 1.0472 | 0.74 |
    | wifi_high (13.5) | 1.0448 | 0.84 |
    | wired_to_cellular (75) | 1.0824 | 0.98 |
    | wired_wired (117, not quotable) | 1.0421 | 1.08 |

    Residuals ≤ 0.11 s everywhere; the 4 MB excess (0.58–0.93 s) sits on each case's b. The slope: tbf shapes frame bytes and the charge counts payload; MTU 1500 framing gives (1500 + 14)/1448 = 1.0456, matching the three quotable non-75 cases to 0.002. The 75 MB/s slope 1.08 is not attributed. The intercept rises with the bottleneck rate, as handshake plus slow start would (slow start must reach the bandwidth-delay product, 0.45 MB at 4 MB/s and about 8 MB at 75 MB/s over an RTT of about 108 ms, roughly 5 vs 9 doublings); this is consistent with the data and is not proven as the mechanism.
  - **Peer-exchange n = 1** (pass: median within 10 % of the charge at 50, 250 and 500 MB). 6 of the 12 required points pass. Measured ÷ charge at 50/250/500 MB: cellular_to_wired 1.09/1.05/1.05 pass; wifi_high 1.28/1.09/1.07 (50 MB fails); wifi_low 1.15/1.07/1.06 (50 MB fails); wired_to_cellular 2.53/1.34/1.24 fails at all three. 4 MB is disclosed, not judged: 1.55 / 3.49 / 2.28 / 9.60, against the charge + 1 RTT +40.9 % / +165.2 % / +89.6 % / +378.3 %.
  - **Wired rows are not quotable under the filed sender rule** (unshaped probe 758 MB/s < 1,170). The wired fit matches the others, but the rule stands.
  - **n > 1** (50 and 250 MB). The makespan is about n × the additive charge (2.1–2.3 at n = 2, 4.2–4.7 at n = 4), so the additive peer charge understates concurrent exchange on one uplink by about n; this measures the idealisation the paper states ("the exchange does not wait for shared links"). The ingress rule (a capacity-1 pipe per link, FIFO) is judged on the makespan against n × hold + latency: 8 of 16 quotable cells within 10 %; the 4 MB/s cellular uplink at 250 MB (1.12, 1.18) and the 75 MB/s path fail. The per-flow split (FIFO vs TCP fairness) is disclosed, not tested. Full per-flow lists in `table.txt`.
  - **24 of 120 runs had TCP retransmits**, flagged and kept.
  - **Caveats.** A warm persistent connection avoids the handshake but not slow-start-after-idle (Linux default `tcp_slow_start_after_idle` = 1). The rates are the simulator's own, not measured links.

It never replaces a filed result.

- 2026-10-20 — **KPA conformance check (ii): our decider follows Knative v0.50.0's except D1 (panic entry un-ceiled), D2 (panic sizing without the stable count), D3 (one-tick un-panic boundary) and D6 (Knative's binary-float ceil at exact multiples of the target), on 1,328 of 15,516 window-full ticks at heavy (8.6 %) and 4,194 of 37,436 at moderate (11.2 %); UNEXPLAINED 0; joint residual 0.** (SCIENTIST's wording; not "conforms".) Knative asks for more on 1,199 of 1,328 and 3,731 of 4,194 differing ticks; ours is about 1.8 % and 3.2 % below in summed desired. Descriptive, no label, open loop.
  - **Decision.** `KpaScaler` is not changed before submission: a change there is a physics change and would invalidate every filed cell (the expert). The paper names D1, D2, D3 and D6 as departures. A closed-loop rerun with Knative's choices applied is the only way to know their latency effect. That is a separate decision.
  - **Design and approvals.** Designed by S6; reviewed by SCIENTIST (cells, linear aggregation only, a defaults-equality row, D8 named, joint residual); accepted by the expert.

Each row is one decision per function per tick, for the four functions.
  - **Knative side.** knative.dev/serving `v0.50.0` (module sum `h1:9XUQq2yqUPN1YZUuGH6nF8IMvuumNvGXzCrSTYu44bA=`), go 1.25.14.
    - Test file `uncut_groups_s0_v1/kpa_conf/kpa_conformance_test.go` md5 `c155f7ed`, with `go.mod` `4598959b` and `go.sum` `bbe09416`. Run as `go test -run TestKpaConformance` with `KPA_TRACE` and `KPA_OUT` set.
    - The decider is the release's own `scaling.New`. Its `DeciderSpec` and windows are built by the release itself: `NewConfigFromMap({})` defaults, a PodAutoscaler with containerConcurrency 1, `MakeDecider` and `MakeMetric`.
    - Observations go into the release's `TimedFloat64Buckets` (60 s and 6 s, 1 s buckets, linear) as `collection.record` does, with proxied = 0. The collector's empty-bucket `ErrNoData` test is kept.
    - Output: per-tick `kpa_conf/go_g0heavy.csv` md5 `f805bf57`, `go_g0moderate.csv` `41b27fc7`.
  - **Defaults-equality row** (`knative_defaults.json` md5 `637b9b1b`). Every field we use equals our constants: target 0.7 (total 1), stable 60 s, panic 6 s (10 %), threshold 2.0, max up 1000, max down 2, scale-down delay 0, 1 s buckets, reachable.
    - Defaults outside the decider are listed, not compared: initial scale 1, target burst capacity 211, scale-to-zero grace 30 s.
    - The Go test ran with activation scale **0**, as `MakeDecider` gives it with no annotation; the design note had said 1. The decider only adjusts counts when activation scale > 1, so 0 and 1 give identical counts.
  - **Our side.** `src/placement/scaleout.py` at reference-physics `5acaecf2` (md5 `0a39560a`). `scaleout.py` and `autoscaler.py` are unchanged between `a459dd98` and `5acaecf2`, so this is the cells' decider.
  - **Comparison.** `uncut_groups_s0_v1/kpa_conf/kpa_conformance.py` md5 `4d81c8a3`. Reports `report_g0heavy.txt` `fba1eef8` and `report_g0moderate.txt` `df157022`; per-tick `go_g0heavy.compare.csv` `08947713` and `go_g0moderate.compare.csv` `8f5327b4`.
    - Self-checks: the replayed `KpaScaler` equals the traced desired on 100 % of ticks.
    - A parametrised port with every switch at "ours" equals `KpaScaler` on 100 % of ticks. With every switch at Knative (**the joint alignment**), it equals the Go series on 100 % of ticks, filling window included: **joint residual 0**.
    - A difference counts as explained only when aligning that mechanism alone (or the smallest combination) removes it. Nothing is fitted.
    - The window is full when t − first sample ≥ 60 s.
  - **Results, window-full ticks.**
    - heavy: 1,328 of 15,516 ticks differ (8.6 %); Knative is higher on 1,199 and lower on 129; median |diff| 4 replicas; ours is 1.8 % below in summed desired.
    - moderate: 4,194 of 37,436 differ (11.2 %); Knative higher on 3,731, lower on 463; median |diff| 3; ours is 3.2 % below in summed desired (1 − 1/1.033).
    - Largest single-tick gaps: +60 (heavy) and +140 (moderate).
    - Per function (window-full ticks: 3,879 per function at heavy, 9,359 at moderate). Columns: ticks explained by each alignment alone, D1–D7; then `multi` (ticks explained by more than one single alignment, so counted in two columns); then `joint-only` (ticks removed only by a combination). Each row closes: differ = the D columns − multi + joint-only.

      | rung | fn | differ | D1 | D2 | D3 | D4 | D5 | D6 | D7 | multi | joint-only | UNEXP |
      |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
      | heavy | cnn | 315 | 155 | 0 | 30 | 0 | 4 | 68 | 0 | 1 | 59 | 0 |
      | heavy | dnn1 | 172 | 115 | 0 | 28 | 0 | 1 | 28 | 0 | 1 | 1 | 0 |
      | heavy | dnn2 | 446 | 277 | 124 | 33 | 0 | 0 | 10 | 0 | 0 | 2 | 0 |
      | heavy | rf | 395 | 174 | 0 | 31 | 0 | 0 | 183 | 0 | 0 | 7 | 0 |
      | moderate | cnn | 779 | 537 | 0 | 81 | 0 | 0 | 150 | 0 | 0 | 11 | 0 |
      | moderate | dnn1 | 887 | 716 | 0 | 59 | 0 | 6 | 47 | 0 | 0 | 59 | 0 |
      | moderate | dnn2 | 1,586 | 868 | 280 | 69 | 0 | 0 | 51 | 0 | 1 | 319 | 0 |
      | moderate | rf | 942 | 569 | 107 | 84 | 0 | 1 | 111 | 0 | 2 | 72 | 0 |
    - **UNEXPLAINED: 0 in every function and rung.**
  - **The mechanisms.**
    - **D1, panic entry.** Knative tests the ceiled count, ceil(panic/0.7)/max(1, ready) ≥ 2; we test the unceiled ratio, so Knative panics earlier. It explains the most ticks alone: heavy 155 / 115 / 277 / 174; moderate 537 / 716 / 868 / 569 (cnn / dnn1 / dnn2 / rf).
    - **D2, desired in panic.** Knative takes max(stable, panic) and holds the running max; we take max(panic, running max, current) and omit the stable count. It explains dnn2 heavy 124; dnn2 moderate 280; rf moderate 107; nothing in the other functions.
    - **D3, un-panic.** Knative needs strictly more than 60 s since the last over-threshold tick; we exit at ≥ 60 s, so we leave one tick early. It explains 28–33 ticks per function at heavy and 59–84 at moderate.
    - **D6, the ceil epsilon.** This is Knative's floating-point behaviour, not a rule of ours. It is recorded as an explained difference, and nothing is to be "fixed" toward it. It is binary floating point, not a rounding-rule choice: a stable average of exactly 2.1 gives 2.1 / 0.7 = 3.0000000000000004, and Knative's ceil makes that 4. Our `ceil(x − 1e-9)` gives 3. So Knative asks for one more replica whenever the average is an exact decimal multiple of the target. It explains heavy 68 / 28 / 10 / 183; moderate 150 / 47 / 51 / 111.
    - **D5, averaging.** It explains at most 6 ticks per function.
      - The tick is 1.000 s apart on all but 4–5 ticks per function (1.001–1.033 s and one 2.0 s gap).
      - Tick times are not whole seconds on 3,858 of 3,938 (heavy) and 7,780 of 9,418 (moderate) ticks per function.
      - Per function: 0 shared buckets and 1 empty bucket. Bucket truncation therefore almost never changes the average.
    - **D4** (hold of the last replica) **and D7** (no data) **fire on 0 ticks.**
    - **Ticks needing two or three alignments together,** counted as explained by combination (SCIENTIST's ruling: the joint residual is 0 and every such tick is removed by ≤ 3 alignments). All are removed by the smallest combination listed: heavy cnn 59 (D1+D3 38, D1+D6 20, D3+D6 1); moderate dnn2 319 (D1+D2 307, D1+D3 4, D2+D3 3, D1+D2+D3 4, D3+D6 1), rf 72 (D1+D2 60, …), dnn1 59 (D1+D2 52, D1+D3 7), cnn 11. They are D2 acting inside panics that only D1 opens, and similar pairings.
  - **Open-loop limit.** This test compares *desired* counts, not *realised* replica counts.
  - **Realised effect, approximate.** Applying each desired count to the traced replica count (scale-ups capped at 120 minus the live total, scale-downs at the idle replicas in the trace's occupancy), the change would differ on 1,325 of 1,328 (heavy) and 4,186 of 4,194 (moderate) differing ticks. Rate limits do not absorb the departures. Not modelled here: memory refusals and A3-protected replicas.
  - **Untested (D8 and the boundary).** Each one is named:
    - **D8, the metric fed to the decider.** Real Knative feeds it a per-second time-averaged concurrency scraped from pods and the activator. We feed the instantaneous in-flight count at the tick. Both sides got the same obs here, so this test cannot see that difference.
    - **D4's reconciler half:** the 30 s scale-to-zero grace and the pod retention.
    - **Initial scale 1** at revision creation (we start from 0).
    - **Pod start-up.**

    - **VOID unless all three hold:**
      - (i) the minted `nonpair_sha256` is equal across the four levels;
      - (ii) the built `{rps, duration, events}` hash (the minter's serialization) is equal across the four levels;
      - (iii) every group carries its k = 2 bytes at every level (rtol 1e-9).
    - **Why not the recorded hash:** the builder rewrites events (rate scale, task mix, single origin, client remap). A built file can therefore never reproduce the minted hash, so (ii) replaces "recomputed = recorded" (SCIENTIST: stronger than the spec).
    - **Admission:** as P10. A rung that is saturated (≥ 1 cell) on every topology is excluded and listed. A partially saturated heavy rung still removes topologies, and can VOID the read through the > 4 clause.

- 2026-10-19 — **Partner-structure study (FL gnn and FL MLP vs the in-flight bar across partners drawn per task, k = 1 / 2 / 4 / 8): REGISTERED before any mint or cell.** Registered by SCIENTIST and relayed by the expert; filed verbatim by S5, with the SCIENTIST review amendments below the signed text. The 12 topologies are the exchange-study ids 30176–30187 ("topologies already read in the exchange study").
  - **Factual corrections to the proposal.**
    - (a) The 12 exchange-study topologies use the wf1_v1 sampler (log-normal, median 4 MB, plus 10 % of pairs at 50–500 MB; `gate_inputs_xs_x1` records sampler wf1_v1). The 200 MB × 10^U line in `grounded_workload_v1_mint.py:14` is the legacy sampler. So "400 MB/pair at degree 1, 50 MB/pair at degree 8" is not what would be compared; see the bytes rule.
    - (b) "Degree 2" is partners drawn per task, not realized degree (`grounded_workload_v1_mint.py:145–150`). Each task draws min(2, size−1) partners and pairs are undirected and deduped, so a task's realized degree is about 2–4. The regroup step (`:151–166`) also adds connector pairs between components. So the knob is k = partners drawn per task, and the read reports realized mean degree and pair count per variant.
  - **Design.**
    - Levels k = 1 / 2 / 4 / 8, partners drawn per task, capped at size − 1. Report the share of tasks in groups of size ≤ k + 1, where k is already all-pairs and structure stops changing.
    - Nested graphs, required: per task, draw one ordered sample of min(8, size−1) partners once; level k uses its first k. Only the pair set changes across levels. Arrivals, types, origins and group membership must be identical across levels. The mint line proves it with one sha256 per topology over the workload with `peer_exchange` removed, equal across the four levels. The current loop interleaves the legacy `payload()` draw with `rng.sample`, so the knob must hold every non-pair draw fixed. Connector pairs (needed mostly at k = 1) are counted and reported.
    - Bytes, held per group, not per pair. Draw per-pair bytes with wf1_v1 as today. Then scale every pair in a group by (that group's total bytes at k = 2) / (its total at level k), so each group carries exactly its k = 2 bytes at every level. This holds load fixed group by group; a per-pair 2/k factor would not, because it moves bytes between small capped groups and large ones. Disclosed side effect: more pairs means more heavy-tier draws before rescaling, so the per-pair size distribution changes. Report the share of bytes in pairs above 50 MB per level.
    - Topologies: the 12 exchange-study ids 30176–30187, labelled "topologies already read in the exchange study". Their ×1 rows are known, so k = 2 is not unseen evidence; the test is paired across levels. k = 2 reuses the xs x1 cells only if its workload files are md5-identical to xs x1. Otherwise all four levels are run new.
    - Cells: windows g0–g3, both rungs, at each level. Reactive (admission), `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` at a459dd98, FL gnn s1–2 and FL MLP s1–2 at 74ddf70e, `_k100`, served unchanged. Whole groups are dispatched together, so there is no extra rendezvous; the read confirms R_rendezvous = 0.
    - Admission as always: reactive ≤ 0.80 per cell. A wholly saturated rung is excluded and listed. Trend ids are those admitted at every level. VOID if any level loses more than 4 ids.
  - **Primary (Holm k = 2).** Page's L, one-sided, over k = 1 < 2 < 4 < 8, on the paired % FL gnn vs FL MLP at moderate load, whole and after-360. Topology value = median over 4 windows of the seed-mean paired %. Exact Page's L when there are no ties; the tied fallback as in P10. Direction: the GNN's lead over the MLP grows with k. DEGREE-TREND iff Holm < .025 on both; else NO-TREND.
  - **Interpretation, filed now.** Both models were trained at k = 2, so k = 4 and 8 are out of distribution, which biases against the registered direction. A DEGREE-TREND is therefore despite the shift. NO-TREND does not show that structure is irrelevant; it may be the shift. Neither label says "message passing".
  - **Descriptive (p unadjusted, never a label), per level.** gnn vs bar, MLP vs bar, gnn vs MLP at both rungs and both windows; realized degree; pairs per task; byte share above 50 MB; exchange-saved ÷ net-gained; charge miss per arm. The paper may use the gnn-vs-bar rows for "the gain holds across partner structures" only as descriptive. The raw-bytes secondary is dropped: ×3 already measures the load lever, and per-group byte matching removes the reason for it.
  - **Registered prior (SCIENTIST, verbatim).** P(DEGREE-TREND) ≈ 0.30. GNN − MLP at ×1 is −0.8 / −2.2 % moderate. A matching (k = 1) should be nearly pointwise-separable, but the out-of-distribution shift at 4 and 8 works against the trend.
  - Signed: SCIENTIST, 2026-10-19.
  - **Amendments before mint (SCIENTIST review of the knob, 2026-10-19; S5 files them as relayed).**
    - Knob `--partners-nested K` in `grounded_workload_v1_mint.py`, `rp/partners-knob`, reviewed at c71262f8 → a6d4a9cc (merged), full suite 2,918 passed, 115 skipped.
    - Levels stay k = 1 / 2 / 4 / 8; no re-levelling. The dry run (job 888692, `partners_prep/dry2-888692.out`) gives realized degree over all tasks 1.72 / 2.96 / 4.71 / 7.06 and pairs per task 0.86 / 1.48 / 2.36 / 3.53 at g0, strictly increasing. The read and the paper label the axis by realized degree, not by k.
    - **R1:** the raw wf1_v1 bytes are drawn once over the sorted union of the level 1–8 pair sets, so a pair present at two levels has the same raw bytes at both and differs only by its group's scale factor.
    - **R2:** degree is reported as mean distinct partners over tasks with at least one partner, with the all-task figure kept as `mean_degree_all_tasks`; the zero-partner share, the singleton share and the share in groups of 2..k+1 are reported; one more byte line gives the share of bytes in groups whose pair set differs from level 2. The minter fails loud on a pair that joins two peer groups.
    - **Level 2 is a new workload** (74,061 vs 74,147 pairs at g0, md5 differs from xs x1), so all four levels run new cells and none reuses the xs x1 cells.
    - **Named confound.** The same bytes are spread over more, smaller transfers: the share of bytes in pairs above 50 MB falls from about 78 % at level 2 to about 64 % at level 8 (g0: 0.811 / 0.776 / 0.707 / 0.638 at levels 1 / 2 / 4 / 8). The exchange study found the heavy pairs carry about half of the GNN's gain over the bar, so a NO-TREND may partly reflect smaller transfers. Matching the heavy-tier share per group as well is infeasible (a group with no heavy pair at level 2 gains one at level k).
    - **Route for the payloads.** The windows are minted with wf1_v1 inside the grounded mint (partners_nested k, per-group rescale to level 2), then pass through the builder with `--payload-sampler legacy` (a pass-through). Three checks before any cell: each window's `peer_exchange` md5 after the builder pass equals the grounded mint's output; the manifest records payload_sampler "wf1_v1 (grounded mint, partners_nested k, per-group rescale to level 2)" and builder sampler "legacy pass-through"; one grep shows the builder wrote no wf1_v1 resample block. If the gate or any reader derives the sampler from the builder's field rather than the manifest, S5 stops and tells SCIENTIST before the mint (a reader change, frozen first).

  - **Physics of ×0 (SCIENTIST's wording, binding for every read and the paper).** ×0 is a ZERO-PAYLOAD variant, not a no-exchange one. The peer table accepts payload ≥ 0; the transfer term is hops × payload / (bottleneck × 1 MiB) = 0 (the only division is by the bandwidth, guarded > 0); but a remote peer still pays its network latency (0.03–0.15 s) and rendezvous is independent of payload, so co-location keeps some value at ×0. The azblob variant (median 28 B, p90 12.7 kB, p99 595 kB at g0 heavy) is effectively the same regime. A learned-arm margin at ×0 must not be read as "exchange is free".

- 2026-10-19 — **Exchange-model sensitivity (FL gnn vs the in-flight bar across peer-exchange payload scales): REGISTERED before any topology is minted and before any cell exists. SCIENTIST's 9-point design and amendments (a)–(f) are in the text; SCIENTIST signs against this commit after a diff check.** (The user's GO, relayed by the expert. S5 writes the sampler change and mints; S4 / S5 run the cells; S6 drafts, builds the P10 reader and reads.) **Claim, and its only claim:** "FL gnn's margin over the in-flight bar grows as the peer-exchange payload grows." It changes no filed label. The FL + λ and twin cells keep node priority.
  - Signed: SCIENTIST, 2026-10-19 — exchange-model sensitivity registered at s6/exchange-registration 4721d545 before any mint or cell (9 points and amendments (a)–(f); mechanical trend-id rule with the 3-level fallback and VOID rule; Page's L exact, one-sided upper, Holm 4; EXCHANGE-TREND on both moderate tests; prior .75 / .6 / .5).
  - **The confound, stated up front (SCIENTIST point 6).** Both learned arms were trained at ×1, so off ×1 the margin also moves through distribution shift; the untrained bar does not suffer this. **The study measures the served model's robustness to the exchange scale, not the mechanism alone.** For the mechanism there is a descriptive gap attribution (exchange seconds saved vs the total gain) per variant.
  - **Variants (point 1), each a mint-time setting of the payload sampler.**
    - **×0:** no exchange; a sanity variant.
    - **×0.1, ×0.3, ×1, ×3:** ×1 is the in-family anchor, run fresh.
    - **heavy-tier 0 % at ×1:** do the gains live in the 50–500 MB pairs?
    - **Slot for a 7th, "azblob",** only if the user accepts the expert's proposal. Per-pair payloads would be resampled from the Azure Functions blob trace 2020 BlobBytes ECDF (inverse CDF on the same payload stream's u, same pair graph). It would be descriptive only and outside the trend, labelled "blob sizes per access, a proxy, not measured function-to-function exchange". The trace file is filed by sha256 before the mint; if it is not local, the user downloads it.
    - Partner degree is dropped: it changes group structure and the label distribution, not the exchange model.
  - **The knob: a code change, reviewed by SCIENTIST before the mint (amendment / fact 1).**
    - Today the `wf1_v1` sampler in `src/placement/workload_payloads.py` draws each pair's bytes: log-normal, median 4 MB, σ 1.2, plus a `HEAVY_SHARE` 0.10 of pairs log-uniform 50–500 MB. Its parameters are module constants, so no knob exists.
    - The change adds sampler parameters `scale` (default 1.0) and `heavy_share` (default 0.10), and `azblob` if accepted. Each is recorded in `payload_sampler_meta`.
    - Every pair still consumes the same two draws from its dedicated `payload_rng` (tier, then value), so variants stay paired pair by pair. `scale` multiplies the drawn bytes; heavy-tier 0 % (`heavy_share` = 0) gives a formerly heavy pair the body value of the same u. ×0 is `scale` 0.
    - A test shows the defaults are bit-identical to the existing `wf1_v1` output on an existing mint.
    - S5 writes it. It is filed with SCIENTIST's review in its own line before the mint, and each variant's mint cfg is frozen by md5 in the mint line.
  - **Topologies (point 2).** 12 FRESH ids above 30160, by the literal scan rule: the lowest free 12-run, from S5's scan rerun just before filing (no ids named here).
    - Minted once per variant on the same 12 ids, arrival windows, groups and pair graph, so variants differ only in payload bytes. Every variant runs on the same ids and arrival seeds, so variants are paired.
    - fl3 is not reused: after the FL + λ and twin reads, its ×1 results for FL gnn and the bar are seen.
    - Disjoint from every list in the FL + λ registration, plus fl3 30149–30160.
    - The recipe is confirmation 2's (`big_groups_inputs.sbatch` at `f8814905`, 24 servers, unscaled M, `--payload-sampler wf1_v1`) plus the variant's sampler parameters.
  - **Admissibility, per variant (point 3, amendment (c)).** Reactive (`reactive` at `a459dd98`) runs first. A topology is admitted for a variant only if all 8 of its reactive cells have queue share ≤ 0.80; unknown is not a pass.
    - ×3 is the risk: `payload_scale_v1` saturated reactive at ×3 in the old seat. **If ×3 fails on more than 4 topologies, ×3 is dropped and disclosed, never replaced by a milder scale.**
    - A failed or VOID variant is disclosed, never re-scaled.
    - **The trend set, mechanically (SCIENTIST's ruling):**
      - trend ids are those admitted at EVERY trend level, and a trend id is never replaced;
      - if ×3 fails admission on more than 4 ids, ×3 is dropped (disclosed) and the trend runs on 3 levels, ×0.1 < ×0.3 < ×1, on the ids admitted at all three;
      - otherwise the 4-level trend uses the ids admitted at all four (n ≥ 8, guaranteed by the ≤ 4 rule);
      - if any of ×0.1 / ×0.3 / ×1 loses more than 4 ids, the trend family is VOID and reported;
      - the non-trend variants (×0, heavy-tier 0 %, azblob) use their own admitted ids.
  - **Arms and cells (fact 3, point 8).** Per variant:
    - FL gnn (`ra_gnn_eng_combfl_nosplit`) s1–2 and FL MLP (`ra_mlp_same_combfl_nosplit`) s1–2, `_k100`, at the FL serving chain confirmation 2 used (w at `74ddf70e`); the filed `-final.pt`, no retraining.
    - The bar `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` at `a459dd98`; reactive at `a459dd98` for admission.
    - No `_kr` and no `cd_k100`. The MLP is descriptive. Every cell must record servers = 24.
    - Size: 6 variants × 12 × g0–g3 × 2 rungs × (4 learned + bar + reactive) ≈ 3,460 cells, about 9 h on 4 nodes. If needed, cut ×0.3 or the MLP, never ×0.
  - **Smoke (amendment (b)).** One FL and one bar cell per variant on 19461 before the grid, ×0 above all. Completion status only.
  - **Seal (amendment (e)).** The cells may run before P10 is frozen. Until it is frozen and signed, only job states and file counts are checked; admission reads `queue_share` only. **(d)** The FL + λ / twin cells keep node priority. A sensitivity cell is rerun only for an infrastructure failure, and logged.
  - **Registered primary: one trend family (point 4, amendment (a)).** The registered direction: the margin is more negative as the payload grows. Per rung × statistic (moderate / heavy × whole / after-360), on the trend ids (admission rule above):
    - **the per-topology value:** the paired FL gnn vs bar % per variant (the cell is the seed-1–2 mean; the topology value is the median over g0–g3 of the paired %);
    - **the test:** Page's L across the ordered variants ×0.1 < ×0.3 < ×1 < ×3 (or ×0.1 < ×0.3 < ×1 if ×3 is dropped), one-sided upper in the registered direction (within a topology, the most negative % gets the highest rank);
    - **the p-value:** exact, from the null distribution of L by convolution over topologies (each topology's k! orderings equally likely, k the number of trend levels). If a topology has tied values, midranks are used and the p comes from the normal approximation, disclosed;
    - **Holm k = 4** over the four tests.
    - ×0, heavy-tier 0 % and azblob are outside the trend.
  - **Labels.**
    - **EXCHANGE-TREND:** Holm < .025 on BOTH moderate tests (whole and after-360). Heavy is not required, and is reported.
    - **NO-TREND:** any other outcome; the four tests are reported.
    - The label stands as read; there is no widening.
  - **Registered prediction, not a gate (point 5).** At ×0, |median FL gnn vs bar| ≤ 1 % on all four tests. A learned-arm win at ×0 would mean the gain is not only exchange, and it is reported prominently.
  - **Descriptive (points 6–7; p unadjusted, never a label; after the primary md5 is filed):**
    - per variant: FL gnn vs the bar (four tests), FL MLP vs the bar, FL gnn vs FL MLP;
    - the gap attribution (exchange seconds saved vs total gain);
    - heavy-tier 0 % vs ×1; azblob if run.
  - **Registered prior (SCIENTIST point 9):** P(EXCHANGE-TREND) ≈ 0.75; P(the ×0 prediction holds) ≈ 0.6 (the GNN may still win a little on queue / cold, or lose OOD); P(×3 admissible) ≈ 0.5.
  - **Reader: P10 `--confirm-exchange-trend`,** on the frozen-reader pattern:
    - the per-variant grid exact (R1);
    - servers = 24;
    - the FL w chain as confirmation 2's;
    - Page's L as above;
    - a staged primary and secondary.
    - Proven by B3 on every read of record. Frozen and signed before any cell is opened.
  - **Process:**
    - (1) SCIENTIST signs;
    - (2) the sampler change, reviewed;
    - (3) the mint, filed per variant;
    - (4) admission, smoke, then the cells, sealed;
    - (5) P10 frozen;
    - (6) B3, then the primary alone;
    - (7) one read; any void is disclosed with what it printed;
    - (8) Rule 6: the label stands as read.

    - The diff from `391a1ec1` is the P2 constant, its comment and one dict entry, plus tests.
    - A unit test shows: without the entry the link fails; as a w pin before a gate change it fails; with the entry the chain is accepted; an admitted link that rewrites a frozen key fails.

  - **Read order.** The twin read runs only AFTER the FL + λ primary md5 is filed, so FL + λ's seal is not opened early.
  - **Primary family: FL gnn vs the twin** (Holm k = 4 per charge, both charges, combined by `weaker_f1`). The tests are moderate and heavy × whole and after-360, on fl3 30149–30160 at 24 servers.
    - The cell value is the mean of seeds 1–2. The topology value is the median over g0–g3 of the paired %. The test is an exact two-sided Wilcoxon.
    - **Seed-matched clauses:** FL seed s vs twin seed s, ≤ −1 % on all four, and collapses also seed-matched.
  - **F2 labels.**
    - **MP-ATTRIBUTED:** FL gnn beats the twin under the full program win rule: median ≤ −2 %, Holm < .025 on all four, each seed ≤ −1 %, collapses seed-matched.
    - **MP-DIRECTION:** FL gnn is faster on all four medians and Holm < .025 on at least two, but the full rule fails.
    - **TWIN-FASTER:** the twin is Holm-faster (< .025) on any test.
    - **TIE:** anything else.
    - **Only MP-ATTRIBUTED licenses a message-passing claim in the paper.** The label stands as read; there is no widening after the read.
  - **Descriptive** (after the primary md5 is filed; p unadjusted, never a label):
    - the twin vs FL MLP: is the GNN without message passing still ahead of the pointwise model (architecture and EdgeScorer vs MP)?
    - the twin vs the bar;
    - the twin at 64 servers on 30129–30140, labelled "topologies already read in size-64".
  - **Registered prior (SCIENTIST):** P(MP-ATTRIBUTED) ≈ 0.15, P(MP-DIRECTION) ≈ 0.20, P(TIE) ≈ 0.55, P(TWIN-FASTER) ≈ 0.10.
  - **Process:**
    - (1) SCIENTIST signs; then the parity test, the md5 file and the host lines;
    - (2) P7b and P9 are frozen and signed;
    - (3) a smoke on 19461, then the cells;
    - (4) B3 at P9;
    - (5) the primary read, alone, after the FL + λ primary md5 is filed;
    - (6) one read; any void is disclosed with what it printed;
    - (7) Rule 6: the label stands as read.
  - **Cost (estimate).**
    - Training about 10 h CPU per seed, in parallel; cells about 1 h wall on 4 exclusive nodes.
    - The parity test, P7b and P9 are a few hours, in parallel with training.

Each is FL's yaml (`uncut_comb_fl_gnn_eng`, `uncut_comb_fl_mlp_same`) with only the lineage, run name, tags and two keys changed: `NEAR_RTT_REGRET_LAMBDA: '2.0'`, and `NEAR_RTT_MOVE_REGRET_PATH` set to `/share/nikola.lukic/uncut_groups/lever_sim/move_regret_simlabels.pkl` (sha256 `0117cbd2ee9af473ad14d0ab3e32b7d08cdd1d54df9ed631da9486628c78cdc2`).
    - **The term.** λ weights the expected move-regret in the batched teacher-forced loss: L = CE(label) + λ · Σ over label steps Σ_c p_c · d_c (`regret_term`, `src/policy/gnn/batched_tf.py`). d_c comes from the table, with 10 for never-scored candidates.
      - It applies to 3,118 of the 9,745 train graphs. Val and test carry no move cost.
      - The MLP uses the same term, with no code change. Its smoke logged a non-zero `[REGRET]` (0.0066).

    - **Admission:** reactive (`reactive` at `a459dd98`) runs first, alone, on all 96 cells. A topology is admitted only if all 8 of its reactive cells have queue share ≤ 0.80. Unknown is not a pass: a missing, failed or watchdog-killed cell, or one without the field, fails the topology.
    - A failed topology is replaced by the lowest free id above the run (S5's rule). The replacement is scanned, minted, admitted and filed in its own line before any other cell runs on it. At most 4 replacements; beyond that the family stops and is reported. No id is dropped for an outcome.
  - **Cells** (one venue: CPU-amd, EPYC 9335, SMT off, `--exclusive`, PAR 60, 1 thread, CELL_MEM 16G, LIMIT 14400; ≥ 4 nodes chosen by `sinfo` load, never os-cpu-slurm-21; `R1A_SLATE=declared_pruning_v1_uncapped`).
    - Reactive: 96.
    - Bars: 192.
    - Learned: each of the four learned arms gets 2 seeds × 2 charges × 12 × g0–g3 × 2 rungs = 384 cells, 1,536 in all, at the w-only pin (P7).
    - In all, 1,824 cells.
    - The primary gate directories hold the bars, FL + λ and FL. The MLP arms are in a separate descriptive directory, which is not read, summarised or listed until the primary md5 is filed.
  - **Primary family: FL + λ vs FL** (Holm k = 4 per charge). The tests are moderate and heavy × whole and after-360, on the 12 new topologies only, never pooled with any other set. Each charge is compared with the same charge: FL + λ `_k100` vs FL `_k100`, and `_kr` vs `_kr`.
    - The cell value is the mean of seeds 1 and 2. The topology value is the median over the 4 windows of the paired %. The test is an exact two-sided Wilcoxon.
  - **Win rule (the program's):**
    - median ≤ −2 % and Holm p < .025 on all four;
    - **seed-matched per-seed clause:** FL + λ seed s alone vs FL seed s, ≤ −1 % on all four, for s = 1 and s = 2. Each λ run shares its control's recipe, corpus, seed and trainer path and differs only in λ; the cell and pooled tests stay on seed means;
    - **seed-matched collapses:** FL + λ seed s ≤ FL seed s at each rung (the reader's count);
    - the anchor clause as filed;
    - read under `_k100` and `_kr` and combined by `weaker_f1`: a win must hold under both.
  - **No B6 cap.** The topologies are fresh and the family is registered before any cell, so a win is program-level.
  - **Labels.**
    - **λ-WIN:** the win rule holds under both charges.
    - **NOT-WIN:** any other outcome; the reader's own label is reported, including whether FL + λ is slower.
    - **Stop rule: the label stands as read.** No widening after the read: no other λ, seed, topology, statistic or subset.
  - **Secondary, descriptive only.** Never a label, never pooled into the primary, p unadjusted; computed after the primary md5 is filed:
    - (a) FL + λ vs `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100`, and vs `cd_k100`;
    - (b) FL MLP + λ vs FL MLP;
    - (c) FL + λ vs FL MLP + λ, a model-class contrast under λ (no MP-OFF twin, so no MP claim);
    - (d) FL MLP + λ vs FL gnn (λ = 0), `_k100`: does the cost term let the pointwise model catch the current GNN?
  - **Registered prior (SCIENTIST, before any FL + λ output or topology).**
    - P ≈ 0.25 that FL + λ beats FL at heavy whole by ≥ 1 pt.
    - P ≈ 0.35 that FL MLP + λ is within 0.5 pt of FL gnn (λ = 0) at heavy whole, i.e. that the cost term lets the pointwise model catch the current GNN.
  - **Prerequisites (each filed in its own line before any learned cell).**
    - **P6, gate change:** adds the kinds `ra_gnn_eng_flreg_nosplit` and `ra_mlp_same_flreg_nosplit` (stems above). Each cell checks the sidecar: batched loss, λ 2.0, the table sha256 `0117cbd2…`. It touches only the gate file and its tests, proven by listing equality against the parent with the new kinds removed.
    - **P7, w-only pin:** the frozen decision-cost w of the two new kinds, calibrated by the filed recipe on one exclusive node pair. The commit changes only the w file. FL's and the FL MLP's w are unchanged, so their cells at P7 serve as at `74ddf70e`.
    - **P8, reader:** `--confirm-fl-lambda`, on the frozen-reader pattern of P4 / P5:
      - the primary contrast is learned vs learned (FL + λ vs FL), not vs the bar, with the per-seed clause and collapses seed-matched to the control;
      - seeds exactly 1, 2; Holm < .025;
      - every cell records servers = 24;
      - the topology guard extended by every list above;
      - R1 grid-or-void: `--gate` must be exactly the bars, FL + λ and FL (960 cells) with 0 failed and nothing extra, and in the secondary `--desc-gate` must be exactly the two MLP arms (768);
      - a staged primary and secondary read.
    - The reader is proven by B3 on every read of record in its filed `--gate` order. It is frozen at the reference-physics commit that carries it and signed before any cell.
  - **Process:**
    - (1) SCIENTIST signs; then the mint, then reactive admission, then every other cell.
    - (2) The checkpoint md5 file and a smoke on 19461 (one FL + λ cell per charge).
    - (3) One read script, written locally and copied.
    - (4) B3 at the frozen P8 reader before the primary.
    - (5) The primary stage runs alone and stops non-zero on failure, VOID included; nothing descriptive is computed until its md5 is filed.
    - (6) One read; any void is disclosed with what it printed.
    - (7) Rule 6: the label stands as read.
  - **Cost (estimate, from confirmation 2's cells).**
    - Training: GNN about 8.6 h, MLP about 5.7 h, in parallel.
    - Mint about 1 min; reactive about 96 × 1–3 min.
    - Learned cells about 1 h wall per 384 on 4 exclusive nodes; bars about 8 min.
    - P6–P8 about 2–3 h, in parallel with training. The read about 1 min.

It is the conf1 FL cells, window g0 only, on the 24 fresh topologies 19707–19730 (`conf/cells_bar`, `conf/cells_learned`; the same cells as the FL confirmation), so its n is 24, not 3. The `read_size` and `mlp_size_read` readers pool it as "24 (conf1, g0)".

  - **Reading.** Both learned arms beat the bar at every size and rung except the 16-server heavy after-360 (gnn +1.17 %, MLP +0.18 %). The gain rises with size (moderate whole gnn −5.2 % at 16, −5.8 % at 24, −14.8 to −20.8 % at 40, −17.5 % at 56, −25.7 % at 64). The MLP carries most of it; the gnn's extra over the MLP is largest at 64 servers moderate (−6.67 % whole, −9.12 % after-360, 3/3) and mixed in sign at heavy. The per-term split of the size gain (peer-exchange time carries it for both arms) is in `mlp_pilot/size_decomp.txt`, not repeated here; no MP-OFF twin was run, so no message-passing claim follows.

- 2026-10-19 — **Per-term latency breakdown (Eq. 2): descriptive, no label.** (S5; filed cells only, no new cell, report-only at the user's GO via the expert; the held conf2 read is untouched.) Whole run, windows g0–g3 pooled, per topology the mean over windows (and over GNN seeds 1–2); delta = GNN − bar; faster/12 counts topologies with GNN below bar; raw two-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank over the 12 topology means (the smallest p at n = 12 is 0.0005; no correction, descriptive). GNN = FL `ra_gnn_eng_combfl_nosplit_k100` s1–2; bar = `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` s0. Sets: (a) the 12 gate topologies 19537–19548 (`comb/cells_comb_fl` vs `comb/cells_bar_g8`), (b) the 12 size-64 topologies 30129–30140 (`size64/cells_learned` vs `size64/cells_bar`; the secondary is filed, so size64 is open).
  - **Result.** Peer exchange is the whole gain: Δ exchange −0.300 / −0.178 s (gate moderate / heavy) and −0.701 / −0.397 s (size 64) against Δ elapsed −0.213 / −0.097 / −0.652 / −0.177 s, 12/12 topologies faster on exchange in every row. GNN pays for it in the scheduler wait (+0.024 to +0.029 s, 0/12 in every row), in the residual queue Q_resid (+0.018 to +0.069 s, 0/12 or 2/12 faster; at size-64 heavy the queue field itself is −0.040 s because pull is −0.060 s) and in the compute-lock wait (+0.020, +0.034 and +0.274 s, with −0.007 s at size-64 moderate). **Co-location share is lower for GNN in all four rows (−3.3, −1.7, −10.4, −4.0 pp) while its exchange seconds are lower**: the exchange saving is not more co-located pairs (the share counts transfers, not seconds; the mechanism, for example cheaper access classes for the remaining transfers, is not measured here).
  - **Field map** (`src/placement/infrastructure.py`, `orchestrator.py`; script docstring):
    - W = `averageWaitTime` (scheduled − dispatched: batch collection plus the charged decision time).
    - queue field = `averageQueueTime` (arrived − scheduled), which holds the FIFO queue, ingress propagation, ingress transfer and link wait, and the image pull. Separable parts: propagation N = `averageNetworkLatency` and pull P = `averagePullTime`; the rest, Q_resid = queue − N − P, is the FIFO queue plus ingress transfer and link wait, **not separable** (no summary field carries ingress wait or link wait).
    - K = `averageColdStartTime`. R = `totalPeerRendezvousWait` / n. X = `totalPeerExchangeTime` / n.
    - IO = `averageCommunicationsTime` − X (input read plus output write, **not separable from each other**; 0.0168 s in every cell, the same on both arms).
    - L = `lock_wait.mean`. E = `averageExecutionTime`. elapsed = `averageElapsedTime`. compute_resid = `averageComputeTime` − (comm + L + E) (output storage wait and the like).
    - Informational: decision wall = `decisionTiming.total_s` / n is real seconds, not simulated time (GNN 0.005–0.006, bar 0.0002–0.0004); the simulated charge sits in W. Cold-start count = `cold_start_pct`/100 × n; scale-ups = the sum of `scaleOut.scale_ups_by_cause`; co-location share = 1 − cross-node transfers (`peerExchangeByAccessClass`) / directed partner relations (2 × `peer_exchange` rows of the window's workload).
  - **Counts.** Replica peak is 120 (gate) / 319 (size 64) on both arms; mean replica count is 1.0 / 1.0 (gate) and 6.7 / 3.1 (size 64) lower for GNN. Cold starts: gate +98 / −6; size 64 +98 (0/12 faster) / −67 (12/12). Tables:

Label: descriptive, no label.
  - **Addendum (2026-10-19): opening only (first 360 s), descriptive, no label.** Same cells, units and tests as above. Opening = tasks dispatched before 360 s (`tasks_bsz` column 1); the script asserts that dispatch ≥ 360 reproduces the summary's `steady_state.after_360s` n and mean in every cell, so the split is the summaries' own.
    - **Only elapsed and lock wait can be split.** The filed artifacts carry, per task, the dispatch and completion times, group size, served batch size and lock wait (`tasks_lock` column 3). Every other Eq. 2 term (scheduler wait, queue, ingress, pull, cold start, rendezvous, exchange, input/output, execution) exists only as a whole-run summary mean, and the summaries carry an after-360 mean for elapsed alone. **Whole minus after-360 cannot be formed for those terms; no opening value is reported for them.** Reported instead: opening elapsed, opening lock wait, elapsed per 60 s dispatch bin, and elapsed per group-size bucket inside the opening.
    - **Where seed 1 loses at 64 heavy.** The opening holds 25.2 % of tasks (12.6 k of 50 k). After 360 s the GNN beats the bar by −0.287 s (12/12; seed 1 −0.280, seed 2 −0.293), so the whole-run loss of seed 1 comes from the opening: seed 1 opening elapsed is **+0.553 s (2/12 faster, p = 0.0068)** against seed 2 −0.274 s (9/12). Inside the opening it sits in the 60–120 s dispatch bin (seed 1 **+5.31 s, 0/12, p = 0.0005**; seed 2 +2.26 s, 0/12; pooled +3.79 s, 0/12), after a win in the first 60 s (pooled −2.00 s, 11/12; seed 1 −0.62 s, 5/12) and a mostly recovered 120–180 s bin (seed 1 −0.69 s, 12/12). The lock wait in the opening is higher for the GNN in every seed (seed 1 +1.50 s, 0/12; seed 2 +0.62 s, 1/12; pooled +1.06 s, 0/12), and by group size seed 1 loses in the groups of at most 10 (+0.52 s, 2/12) and of 11–50 (+0.80 s, 1/12) but not in the bigger groups (−0.01 and +0.05 s). The after-360 lock wait carries no such difference (+0.005 s, 5/12, p = 0.85).
    - **Other rows.** Gate moderate: opening Δ −0.437 s (9/12; seed 1 −0.160, 9/12, p = 0.27), the opening lock wait higher (+0.247 s, 3/12) and a loss in the 180–240 s bin (+0.604 s). Gate heavy: seed 1 opening +0.230 s (5/12, p = 0.09), seed 2 −0.648 s (12/12), the 60–120 s bin +1.70 s for seed 1 (0/12). Size-64 moderate: the GNN is ahead in the opening (−1.16 s, 12/12) apart from the 60–120 s bin (seed 1 +2.83 s, 0/12). Seed 1 loses the 60–120 s bin in three of the four rows (gate heavy, size-64 moderate and heavy; at gate moderate it is −0.27 s, 7/12, and its loss there is the 180–240 s bin, +0.88 s, 2/12); this is a description of the cells, no mechanism is tested.

    - **Mint (frozen by hash):** S5's recipe (`big_groups_inputs.sbatch` at `f8814905`, the confirmation recipe) with **servers = 64** and the arrival multipliers scaled by **64 / 24**: `M_MODERATE` 11.6139 × 64/24, `M_HEAVY` 27.622665025860204 × 64/24. **The group arrival rate is scaled, never the group size.** Windows g0–g3, both rungs, whole-group `inputs_group` tree, a new root.
    - The generator cfg and the sbatch are frozen by md5 in the mint's Record line, together with the ids, the list's sha256 and the MD5SUMS, before any cell. Admission checks are the mint's own (cfg and window files, window rates scaled by 64/24).
    - **The mint line records servers = 64 counted from the minted cfgs** (one grep count, 12 × `"servers": 64`). A copied selector file is not evidence: the pilot's `sel.json` says `"servers": 24` although its cfgs say 64. The expert checked the pilot's own cell summaries: the `servers` field reads 64 for 30121–30123, 40 for 30022 / 30023 and 16 for 30011 / 30012, so the pilot ran at those sizes and only `sel.json`'s label was wrong.
  - **Admissibility, per cell (SCIENTIST's condition 2).** Reactive (`reactive`, the fixed in-flight `knative_network`, seed 0, at `a459dd98`) runs first, alone, on the 12 × 4 × 2 = 96 cells.
    - A topology is admitted only if every one of its 8 reactive cells has queue share ≤ 0.80. **Unknown is not a pass:** a missing, failed or watchdog-killed reactive cell, or one without the field, fails it.
    - A failed topology is replaced by the lowest free id above the run (S5's shortfall rule): re-scanned, minted, admitted, filed in its own line before any other cell on it. At most 4 replacements; beyond that the family stops and is reported. No id is dropped for an outcome.
    - **The admission script reads only the reactive cells' queue share field** and prints pass or fail per topology. It is the one exception to the seal.
  - **Cells** (one venue: CPU-amd, EPYC 9335, SMT off, `--exclusive`, PAR 60, 1 thread, CELL_MEM 16G, LIMIT 14400; ≥ 4 nodes by `sinfo` load, never os-cpu-slurm-21), after admission:
    - **Bar** (`size64/cells_bar`): `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` and `cd_k100`, seed 0, at the G8 base pin `a459dd98` (192 cells).
    - **Primary** (`size64/cells_learned`): FL gnn (`ra_gnn_eng_combfl_nosplit`) seeds 1 and 2, the filed `-final.pt` (md5 from `comb/md5_comball.json`, no retraining), `_k100` and `_kr` (k = 1.468), at the P2 pin `74ddf70e` (384 cells).
    - **Descriptive** (`size64/cells_desc`, never in the primary read):
      - the k = 0 (no-charge) cells of both arms: FL gnn seeds 1–2 as the bare kind at `74ddf70e` (192) and `cd_ib0_inflightfull` at `a459dd98` (96);
      - FL MLP (`ra_mlp_same_combfl_nosplit`) seeds 1–2, `_k100`, at `74ddf70e` (192).
    - Reactive (`size64/cells_reactive`, 96) is the admission set.
    - In all, 1,152 cells.
  - **Primary family (Holm k = 4 per charge).** FL gnn vs `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100`, moderate and heavy × whole and after-360, the reader's statistic on the 12 size-64 topologies only, never pooled with any 24-server set.
    - The cell is the mean of seeds 1 and 2; the topology value is the median over the 4 windows of the paired %; the test is an exact two-sided Wilcoxon.
  - **Win rule, as filed for the program:**
    - median ≤ −2 % and Holm p < .025 on all four;
    - each of seeds 1 and 2 alone ≤ −1 % on all four;
    - collapses ≤ the bar's, per seed (the reader's count, the worse seed's), at each rung;
    - the anchor clause as filed.
    - Read under `_k100` AND `_kr`, combined by `weaker_f1`: the win must hold under both (SCIENTIST's condition 3).
  - **Labels.**
    - **SIZE-64-CONFIRMED (size chosen after an exploratory pilot):** the win rule holds under both charges. It is a claim about 64 servers only and lifts nothing at 24 servers.
    - **NOT-CONFIRMED:** any other outcome; the reader's own label is reported.
  - **Exact grid or VOID (SCIENTIST's condition 2, as R1).**
    - The primary read stops "VOID" unless `--gate` (`cells_bar`, `cells_learned`) is exactly the registered grid (FL s1–2 × `_k100` / `_kr` × 12 × g0–g3 × 2 rungs, plus the two bars: 576 cells) with 0 failed and nothing extra.
    - The secondary stops "VOID" unless `--desc-gate` is exactly its 480 cells.
    - A watchdog kill is a failed cell and is never rerun.
  - **Decision cost at 64 servers (SCIENTIST's condition 3; the measured number replaces the w caveat).** w was calibrated on 24-server batches. Decision wall time only, from existing cells (confirmation 1's 24-server cells; the pilot's 64-server cells, 12 FL and 6 bar), EPYC 9335, SMT off; median ms per plan evaluation by batch-size bucket:

    | | ≤ 10 | 11–50 | > 50 |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | FL, 24 servers | 6.16 | 1.76 | 1.81 |
    | FL, 64 servers | 5.76 | 1.98 | 2.17 |
    | FL, 64 / 24 | 0.94× | 1.12× | **1.20×** |
    | bar, 24 servers | 0.0094 | 0.0097 | 0.0096 |
    | bar, 64 servers | 0.0074 | 0.0085 | 0.0086 |
    | bar, 64 / 24 | 0.79× | 0.88× | 0.90× |

    - FL's per-evaluation cost at 64 servers is at most 1.20× its 24-server cost; the bar's is 0.79–0.90×.
    - So the 24-server w undercharges FL at 64 by up to 20 % and overcharges the bar by 10–21 %. **Both biases favour FL.** The size of both biases is read from the k = 0 descriptive cells; at a felt share of about 1 %, a 20 % undercharge is about 0.2 pp, an order below the −2 % line.
  - **Serving check before any read (SCIENTIST's condition 4; logs only, never a summary or npy).**
    - From one FL and one bar cell log of the new mint, grep only the serving lines (`[PREFIX SERVING]`, the contract source, `[QUEUE FEATURES]`, the slate rule): FL at 64 hits no candidate cap, truncation, size-free-block or sidecar-contract difference from 24 (`platform_cap=0`, the same contract and queue-feature lines).
    - Both arms serve the same candidate rule (`R1A_SLATE=declared_pruning_v1_uncapped`, no cap).
    - The serving check also counts 64 server nodes in one FL cell's infrastructure (a count, not a result).
    - A pilot log pair (30121 at 64 vs 19707 at 24) already shows identical serving lines.
  - **Seal (the expert's rule).** Until the P5 freeze line below is filed, nobody opens any size-64 cell summary, npy or result file. Only job states and file counts are checked, against the registered grid. The exceptions are the admission script (reactive queue share only) and the serving check (serving log lines only). The read runs only on the frozen P5 reader.
  - **Descriptive, never a label and never pooled into the primary (secondary stage, after the primary md5 is filed):**
    - (a) k = 0: FL gnn vs `cd_ib0_inflightfull`, both uncharged;
    - (b) FL MLP vs the bar, and FL gnn vs FL MLP, p unadjusted;
    - (c) gap attribution by bucket;
    - (d) reactive as a row.
  - **Prerequisite P5 (reader; code with tests, before any read).** The frozen reader `13c9343d` cannot read this family: in plain mode the B6 cap closes FL's program-level label, and there is no grid check or staged descriptive gate. P5 adds `--confirm-size64`:
    - seeds exactly 1,2; FL primary; Holm < .025;
    - the labels above;
    - the topology guard extended by 19707–19730, 19953–19976, 19461 and 30011–30123;
    - the R1 grid check on `--gate`;
    - a staged primary and secondary read, with `--desc-gate` in the secondary only (k = 0 cells loaded as bare kinds, the FL MLP `_k100`).
    - It touches only the new mode's path and is proven by B3 on all 13 reads of record, in their filed `--gate` order.
    - The reader is frozen at the reference-physics commit carrying P5, in its own line, signed, before any read.
  - **Process:**
    - (1) mint, then the reactive admission cells and admission, then every other cell;
    - (2) the serving check;
    - (3) one read script, written locally and copied;
    - (4) B3 at the frozen P5 reader before the primary;
    - (5) the primary stage runs alone and stops non-zero on failure (VOID included); nothing descriptive is computed until its md5 is filed;
    - (6) one read; any void is disclosed with what it printed;
    - (7) Rule 6: the label stands as read.
  - **Cost (estimate).** Mint about 1 min; reactive about 96 cells × 1–3 min; the other 1,056 cells about 2–3 h wall on 4 exclusive nodes (64-server cells run longer than 24-server ones); P5 about 1–2 h, in parallel with the cells; the read about 1 min.

  - **Prerequisite (P4, reader; code with tests, before any cell).** The frozen reader `4441fa39` cannot read this family: `--confirm` requires `--seeds 1,2`, `gap_attribution` averages exactly two seeds, F2-MC uses one seed set for both arms, and the used-topology guard lacks 19707–19730.

    - P4 also takes the family's Holm threshold (.0125, C1). A unit test shows `--confirm2` refuses any seed set other than {3,4,5,6} and any guarded topology.
    - It leaves every filed read byte-identical. B3 also shows that `--confirm --seeds 1,2` still requires exactly that set and reproduces `83fa5b8e` and `2d60fba3` byte-identically. This is proven by B3 on read_combfl (50cb302a), the Arm 1b read (301b11c3), both first-confirmation reads (83fa5b8e, 2d60fba3) and the seed-extension reads, in their filed `--gate` order.
    - The reader is then frozen at the reference-physics commit carrying P4, filed in its own line before any cell.
    - No gate change is expected: P1 serves `_kr` by kind, not seed. Whether the stager serves seeds 3–6 from the new md5 file is not yet verified. The smoke on 19461 g0 heavy (one seed-3 cell per charge) checks it before any confirmation cell; any change it needs is a separate, filed prerequisite.
  - **Process.** As the first confirmation:
    - (1) Smoke on 19461 only.
    - (2) One read script, written locally and copied.
    - (3) B3 on the filed reads before the primary.
    - (4) The primary stage runs alone and stops non-zero on failure. Nothing secondary or descriptive is computed until the primary md5 is filed.
    - (5) One read; any void is disclosed with what it printed.
    - (6) Rule 6: the label stands as read.
  - **Cost (estimate, from the first confirmation's cells).**
    - Training: 4 × about 10.5 h CPU on 6 cores, in parallel.
    - Cells: about 1,536 + 1,536 learned at about 8 core-min, plus 384 bar at 5.5 core-min, ≈ 445 core-h, about 2–3 h wall on 6 exclusive nodes.
    - The mint is about 40 s.

    - (P3) A reader change that (a) reads F2-MC, FL gnn vs FL MLP, for a primary with no twin; (b) classifies cells by kind and w-pin rather than by directory, closing the limitation in the entry below; and (c) leaves every filed read byte-identical. Proven by B3 on `read_combfl.json` (50cb302a) and the filed Arm 1b read (301b11c3).
    - **The reader is frozen at the reference-physics commit that carries P3**, filed in its own line before any cell. If P3 turns out not to be needed for (a), the frozen reader is `54e1ad78`.
  - **Cells.** 24 topologies × g0–g3 × moderate and heavy, at a new gate root. All cells run on one venue: CPU-amd, AMD EPYC 9335, SMT off, `--exclusive`, PAR 60, 1 thread, CELL_MEM 16G, LIMIT 14400. Jobs are spread over at least 4 nodes, none on a node whose H3 stop has fired, chosen by `sinfo` load at submit.
    - Bar: `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` and `cd_k100` (robust bar and collapse reference) at the G8 base pin `a459dd98`, seed 0; 192 cells each.
    - FL gnn and FL MLP: seeds 1 and 2, `_k100` and `_kr`, at the P2 pin; 768 cells per kind.
    - In all, 1,920 cells.
  - **Primary family (Holm, k = 4).** FL gnn vs `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100`: moderate and heavy × whole and after-360, the reader's statistic on the 24 new topologies only. The cell is the mean of seeds 1 and 2; the topology value is the median over the 4 windows of the paired %; the test is an exact two-sided Wilcoxon. These 24 are never pooled with the 12 or the 36.
  - **Win rule, as filed for the program.** Median ≤ −2 % and Holm p < .025 on all four; each seed alone ≤ −1 % on all four; collapses ≤ the bar's at each rung. It is read under the frozen verdict (`_k100`) and the co-tenancy verdict (`_kr`, k = 1.468), combined by `weaker_f1`. A win must hold under both.
  - **F2-MC (its own Holm, k = 4).** FL gnn vs FL MLP, the same four tests, the same cells and both verdicts combined. The MODEL-CLASS rule as filed: MODEL-CLASS-ATTRIBUTED only if the primary is CONFIRMED and the GNN is ≤ −2 % with Holm p < .025 on all four; else MODEL-CLASS-DIRECTION (median negative on all four) or NOT-SEPARATED. The label names the corpus (11,073 states, 49 topologies, simulator labels).
  - **Labels.**
    - **CONFIRMED (program-level):** the win rule holds under both verdicts. The B6 cap on FL is then lifted, for FL gnn and this family only. FL is reported as confirmed on fresh topologies, and the 12-topology read stays as filed.
    - **NOT-CONFIRMED:** any other outcome. The reader's own label (MIXED / TIES / E-FASTER) is reported, and FL stays WIN-ARM-ONLY.
    - F2-MC is labelled separately and never changes the primary label.
  - **Process.**
    - (1) **Smoke on a non-confirmation topology only** (19461 g0 heavy, one cell per kind and charge), to check that the plan diffs and the `_kr` charge match k × w. No confirmation cell is read before the cell set is complete and fixed.
    - (2) **One read script, written locally and copied, never typed over `ssh`.** It runs the primary first and **stops (non-zero exit, no further output) if the primary read fails**. **No secondary output (F2-MC, descriptive lines, pooled lines) is computed or printed until the primary read file exists and its md5 is filed.**
    - (3) **One read.** A bug in a tool is fixed and the check rerun; a second failure of the same check is reported before anything else runs. Any read that is void is disclosed with what it printed.
    - (4) **Rule 6:** the family is read whatever any other result says, and the label stands as read.
  - **Cost (estimate, from the filed cells).** Learned cells about 8 core-min each (1,536 cells ≈ 205 core-h), bar cells about 5.5 core-min (384 ≈ 35 core-h); about 240 core-h, roughly 1–1.5 h wall on 4 exclusive nodes. The mint is about 40 s on 8 CPUs. P1–P3 are code with tests, and no training is needed.

Job 884616 went further: its primary read failed on the gate-pin rule, and the job then computed and printed the whole TE3 family and every descriptive contrast above, FL gnn vs FL MLP included, before any primary read existed. These looks cannot have steered F1. F1's inputs are the FL cells and the bar cells only; the cells are byte-identical to the void ones (item 2); and the only change made after the looks was the FL-only gate view, which removes no F1 input. They are still looks at secondary and TE3 results before the primary, and every report of this read lists them as such.
    - **The FL-only gate view works around a reader limitation.** The reader decides which cells are hand cells by directory, not by kind, so the F, L and MLP cells in the same directory were held to the gate pin and refused. The FL cells' code is still checked through the w-pins, and the bar cells at the gate pin. The reader should recognise learned kinds by their w-pin; until it does, any read that shares a directory needs the same view.
    - **No co-tenancy verdict.** The combined-lever registration has no `_kr` cells and the read ran `--cotenancy off`, unlike the program rule for Arms 1, 1b and 2. From the filed linearity check (about 0.468 × the felt share, which is about 1.2 % at heavy), a co-tenancy charge would cost about 0.6 pp. That would leave seed 1's heavy whole-run result at about −1.35 %, still within the −1 % seed rule. This is an estimate, not a measurement.

    - **Where the gain comes from (descriptive, two seeds).** Against Arm 1b gnn, L alone gives most of FL's gain (−3.09 % / −4.91 % on the whole run at moderate / heavy), and F alone gives about none (−0.15 % / +0.57 %). The "together, not apart" reading above is likewise descriptive.
    - **The MLP gets most of the gain at moderate load.** The FL MLP is −5.13 % / −6.68 % against the in-flight bar at moderate (12/12 each), and FL gnn is ahead of it by only −0.44 % / −0.93 % (9/12, 10/12; unadjusted). Any report must say that a pointwise MLP with the same features and labels obtains most of the gain at the moderate load, that the GNN's lead over it is under 1 % there (−3.2 % / −2.0 % at heavy), descriptive, with no model-class claim, and that nothing is credited to graph structure.

      - **(L) current features, simulator labels.** Every train and val state of the corpus is relabelled by the label screen's simulator rule: all plans replayed when the plan space is ≤ 600, else the 8 best distinct plans by S plus the current label. The label is the plan with the lowest replayed group RTT (`uncut_ils.replay_rtts`).
      - **(FL) both.**
      - **FL MLP:** the same-input MLP (`ra_mlp_same`, Arm 1b's MLP config) on FL's features and labels, as the attribution control.
    - **Seeds 1 and 2 for each.** That is 6 GNN runs plus 2 MLP runs, 8 in all. The expert's message counted "8 FL/F/L trainings and 2 MLPs"; 3 arms × 2 seeds gives 6 GNNs.
    - **No twins** (the user's ruling).
  - **Before any training (fail loud, never approximated).**
    - (i) **Feature parity.** On captured live states at the gate pin, the in-flight features the cache gives equal the ones served, to 1e-9. If the stored corpus states lack what the in-flight count needs, F and FL are blocked and reported, not approximated.
    - (ii) **Label audit.**
      - The relabel replays each state's stored current label, and a relative difference above 1e-9 from its stored replayed RTT stops the relabel.
      - The relabel's reader reports, per bucket, the share of labels changed and the mean replayed gain.
      - The 57 accel test ids and the 12 gate topologies' states are never touched.
    - (iii) **Recache determinism.** Two recaches of the same inputs are byte-identical.
  - **Label screen (job 883677).** The user said "go combined" before its read.
    - If it reads LABELS-AGREE, its registered stop is **overridden by the user's direction**, and L and FL still train. The screen's label is still recorded as read.
    - If it reads PROCEED-TO-TRAIN, nothing changes.
  - **Gate.**
    - Gate pin: a new gate change **G8**, a child of the reference-physics line that carries the in-flight bar. It adds the kinds `ra_gnn_eng_{f,l,fl}_nosplit` and `ra_mlp_same_fl_nosplit`, and serves the in-flight feature contract when the sidecar names it.

    - Frozen w for F, L, FL and the FL MLP: calibrated by the learned-w venue recipe (exclusive CPU-amd pair, SMT off, H3 drift rule 2 %, provisional then pooled), on a w-only pin.
    - The bar: `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` (S4's 96 cells, job 883737).
    - For heavy, also TE3's 36 topologies, which need `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` heavy cells there first. They run before any FL TE3 cell is read.
    - **Rule 6:** the live gate runs whatever the label screen, the parity check or any offline number says.
  - **Primary family (Holm, k = 4).** FL gnn vs `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` at moderate and heavy × whole and after-360 on the 12 topologies, with the reader's statistic.
    - **WIN-ARM-ONLY** needs the program's win rule on all four tests: median ≤ −2 %, Holm p < .05, each seed alone ≤ −1 %, and collapses ≤ the bar's.
    - Both statistics must clear at both rungs; otherwise the reader's labels apply (MIXED / TIES / LOSS).
  - **TE3 heavy family (Holm, k = 2).** FL gnn vs `cd_ib0_inflightfull_k100` at heavy whole and after-360 on the 36 TE3 topologies, labelled with the seed extension's per-test labels (CLAIM / REVERSED / CLEAN-NEGATIVE / UNRESOLVED, margin 1 %).
  - **Secondary (descriptive, no label).**
    - FL vs Arm 1b gnn; F vs Arm 1b gnn; L vs Arm 1b gnn; FL gnn vs FL MLP.
    - Arm 1b gnn uses seeds 1 and 2 from its filed cells, plus a descriptive line with the extension seeds 11–18.
    - Each line gives the median, n ahead and an unadjusted p.
