- 2026-10-17 — **Simulator-label screen: REGISTERED before any state is replayed.** (S7; the user's "go screen", relayed by the expert, who fixed the design. **MYOPIC-REPLAY, INTERIM-DESCRIPTIVE**, as with W3: the replay is the group's own tasks from the captured state, not live latency. No training.) **Question:** would labels picked by the simulator differ often enough, and by enough, from the current argmin-S / ILS labels to be worth retraining on?
  - **States.** 500 from the Arm 1 TRAIN key (9,745 states of the 11,073-dataset corpus; the split `experiments/uncut_arm1_split.json`, sha256 613c8cbb…), stratified by group size: ≤ 10 (frame 3,916), 11–50 (3,754), 51–100 (1,618) and 101–256 (457) tasks, **125 each** (every bucket has more than 125). Seed **20261017**: `random.Random(20261017)`, buckets in that order, `sample(sorted dataset ids, 125)`, the draw sorted within each bucket. The draw reads only dataset headers (no replayed RTT, no label outcome): `scripts_cosim/uncut_label_screen.py draw` (6 tests in `tests/test_uncut_label_screen.py`); the list is `docs/lineages/uncut_groups_s0_v1/label_screen_draw.json`, **state-list sha256 `d2a7551a9c5ecc3b7f3a098e8a9ea9929b7c8522781bc63f9c524a801f9fa03d`** (sha256 of the 500 dataset ids joined by newlines in draw order). Never used, and the draw fails loud on them: the 12 gate topologies 19537–19548, the 36 TE3 ids 19554–19589, the 57 `accel_replica_v1` test ids, and the Arm 1 split's own `test` key (the draw takes only `train`). The 500 states sit on 43 corpus topologies, 16201–16249. 67 of the 500 have a plan space ≤ 600 (66 of ≤ 10 tasks, 1 of 11–50).
  - **Current label.** The stored label plan of each state (exact argmin-S, the ILS plan, or the hand plan, per `label_source`). It is replayed again in the same pass so that every plan in a state comes from one code path, and its replayed RTT is compared with the one stored in `placements.jsonl` (a relative difference above 1e-9 on any state stops the screen and is reported).
  - **Small states (plan space ≤ 600, the header's `plan_space_size`).** Replay every plan of the state from the captured state; the simulator label is the plan with the lowest replayed group RTT (the label for the group is the sum over the batch of doneTime − scheduledTime, `uncut_ils.replay_rtts`, the replay the label check uses).
  - **Large states (above 600).** Replay the 8 best distinct plans by S plus the current label (9 or fewer distinct plans); the simulator label is the fastest of them. The 8 best come from the stored ILS trajectory plus the final incumbent: **the corpus does not store replayed RTTs for ILS incumbents** (checked on `ds_00006`: `ils_evaluated.jsonl.gz` rows carry `eval`, `iter`, `S` and `plan` only, and `placements.jsonl` carries replayed rows for the label and, for an ILS label, the hand plan), so nothing is reused and all replays are new. Every ILS- or hand-labelled state in the draw has its `ils_evaluated.jsonl.gz`. The 43 exact-labelled states with a plan space above 600 (≤ 10 tasks, ≤ 100,000 plans) have no trajectory; their 8 best are the 8 lowest-S plans of the full enumeration with the header's S pin (ties by plan order).
  - **Metrics per bucket.** (i) The share of states where the simulator label differs from the current label, a difference meaning a replayed RTT lower than the current label's by more than a relative 1e-9 (equal-RTT alternatives count as agreeing). (ii) The mean and median replayed gain, (RTT(current) − RTT(sim)) / RTT(current), over all states of the bucket (zero where they agree), with a 95 % percentile bootstrap interval of the mean over states (10,000 resamples, seed 20261017). (iii) The share of states with gain ≥ 5 %. By construction the gain is never negative.
  - **Decision rule.** **PROCEED-TO-TRAIN** if, in any bucket, (i) ≥ 20 % and the mean gain ≥ 2 % with a 95 % interval that excludes 0. Otherwise **LABELS-AGREE**: it is recorded and we stop, with no training. This is a screen on myopic replay; **if training goes ahead, the registered live gate runs whatever the offline numbers say** (rule 6).
  - **Cost and venue.** Timed first on 20 states (the first 5 of each bucket in draw order); if the projection for the 500 exceeds 12 CPU-hours, it is reported to the expert before the full run. Any CPU-amd node other than slurm-9, -10 and -20 to -24, with the account queue at or below 44; no node shared with a training run of the program. The replay tooling is the existing one (`uncut_ils.decision_pass`, `uncut_ils.replay_rtts`, the K1b and W3 replay paths); the only new code is the state draw, the per-state plan lists and the reader, in `scripts_cosim/` with tests.
  - **Written before data.** No state has been replayed. The numbers above that come from the corpus headers are sampling-frame facts, not label outcomes.
- 2026-10-17 — **Load descriptors of the moderate rung (descriptive; no ruling changes).** (S7, for the paper draft's description of the moderate load; the expert's request; SCIENTIST's read-only extraction of two of the numbers is reproduced.) Source: the 48 moderate cells of the 869828 hand bar (`cd_ib0_k100_s0.summary.json`, 12 gate topologies 19537–19548 × g0–g3, bar pin d6ae87c2) in `/share/nikola.lukic/uncut_groups/arm1_k1_bar_d6ae87c2`. Median (min–max) over cells; per-cell values in `final_reads/load_descriptors_bar869828.json`.
  - **Queue share of mean task latency:** `queue_share` (averageQueueTime / averageElapsedTime) 0.203 (0.144–0.292); `effective_queue_share` ((averageQueueTime + `lock_wait.mean`) / averageElapsedTime) 0.347 (0.236–0.457). Mean latency (averageElapsedTime) 3.08 s (2.53–3.67). Both SCIENTIST numbers reproduce; the two summary keys equal their recomputation from the components (max abs difference 1.1e-16).
  - **What `queue_time` is (clarification, SCIENTIST and PUBLISHER, 2026-10-17).** `queue_time` = `arrived_time − scheduled_time` (`src/placement/infrastructure.py:437` at d6ae87c2). `arrived` fires only after the platform loop has waited for the replica to be initialised (`yield self.initialized`), popped the task from the platform FIFO (`:1472`), and the task has paid its own network latency (`:1499`) and its ingress link pipes and hold (`:1551`, `:1587`); `task.arrived.succeed()` is at `:1614` (the immediate `arrived.succeed()` at `:1478` is the warm-up fast-forward branch only). So `queue_share` and `effective_queue_share` are not pure platform queueing: they include the FIFO wait, the request's own ingress latency and transfer, and replica start-up, and `effective_queue_share` adds the compute-lock wait on top. "No-queue" below means the latency without these terms, and the split between them was not measured.
  - **Latency over the no-queue service time:** 1.61 (1.33–1.98), where the service time is `averageColdStartTime + averageExecutionTime + averageCommunicationsTime` = 1.93 s (1.40–2.25) (cold 0.133, exec 0.076, transfer 1.73). With `averagePullTime` added to the service time: 1.50 (1.26–1.81); with all queueing (queue + lock wait) removed from the latency: 1.53 (1.31–1.84). The definition changes the figure, so the headline names the first.
  - **Arrival and cluster:** 5.345 tasks/s (5.339–5.346) = `num_tasks` 50,000 / `arrival_end.last_arrival_s`; 24 servers and 160 clients in every cell.
  - **Utilisation (platform compute occupancy), measured by a rerun.** Job 883559 reran the 48 moderate cells at d6ae87c2 with the gate's own `KEEP_RAW_DIR` switch (no gate change; `r1a_gate.sbatch` as launched for 869828, arm `cd_ib0_k100`, `inputs_group`, `arm1_selected.json`); **every rerun cell reproduces its stored summary exactly** (`total_rtt`, `endTime`, both queue shares; 48/48, 0 mismatches, 0 failed). Job 883587 read the platform results from the kept raw files. Definition: a platform's occupation is `100 − idleProportion` = 100 · `load_time` / `env.now` (capacity 1), and `load_time` accumulates `cold_start_duration + task_duration` when a task finishes on it, so transfers are not in it (`src/placement/infrastructure.py:1946`, `:910`, `:925`; `averageOccupation` is the mean over all platforms, `src/placement/orchestrator.py:368`, `:373`). Platforms: 919 per cell, 799 on the 160 clients (which run nothing under `HEROSIM_SERVER_ONLY_REPLICAS`) and **120 on the 24 servers** (8 pynqFpga, 32 rpiCpu, 64 xavierCpu, 8 xavierDla, 8 xavierGpu), the same in all 48 cells. Median (min–max) over cells: `averageOccupation` over all 919 platforms 0.122 % (0.094–0.140); **mean over the 120 server platforms 0.935 % (0.722–1.071)**; over the server platforms that ran something (107, range 90–120) 1.06 % (0.75–1.37); the busiest server platform 8.5 % (6.3–17.6). The busiest types are xavierDla and xavierGpu (about 6 %). Reading, descriptive only: platform compute occupancy is about 1 %, and the no-queue latency is mostly transfer (1.73 of 1.93 s), which is not platform busy time. Per-cell values are in `final_reads/load_descriptors_bar869828.json`; the raw results were removed after extraction (a rerun takes 5.6 min). Not a gate, and it changes no verdict.

    - The model per topology is μ + N(0, σ_T²) + N(0, σ_S²/S) for S new seeds. It simulates the exact Wilcoxon at p < .05/k (Bonferroni, conservative against Holm) and the bound below.
    - Both σ come from two seeds × 12 topologies, so they are rough; σ_T is floored at 0 where it came out negative.
    - Power to claim at the observed mean / probability of a clean negative if the true effect is 0, n = 12:

    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | (a) | Arm 3 − Arm 1b gnn, moderate whole / after-360 | 0.9 / 1.4; 1.6 / 1.2 | .99 / .97 | 1.0 / .98 | 1.0 / .99 |
    | (a) | same, heavy whole / after-360 (clean negative) | 0 / 4.4; 0 / 8.5 | .12 / .05 | .27 / .11 | .36 / .12 |
    | (b) | Arm 1b gnn − twin, moderate whole / after-360 (clean negative) | 0 / 1.7; 0 / 1.6 | .56 / .59 | .94 / .96 | .98 / .98 |
    | (b) | same, heavy whole / after-360 (clean negative) | 2.2 / 1.9; 3.8 / 5.3 | .15 / .05 | .19 / .06 | .18 / .09 |
    | (c) | heavy vs `cd_ib0`: Arm 3, Arm 1b, K0 whole | 4.6–5.3 / 2.5–3.5 | ≤ .06 | ≤ .06 | ≤ .07 |
    | (c) | same, after-360 | 7.2–7.9 / 4.0–5.2 | .14 / .16 / .62 | .17 / .17 / .66 | .16 / .20 / .69 |
    | (d) | Arm 2 − K0 gnn, heavy whole / after-360 (K0 faster) | 0 / 5.3; 0 / 10.8 | .42 / .35 | .94 / .89 | .97 / .97 |
    | (d) | K0 − Arm 1 gnn, heavy after-360 | 3.9 / 7.8 | .34 | .58 | .64 |

  - **What seeds can and cannot do.**

    - (b) at moderate load becomes a clean negative at S = 6–8.

  - **Recipe.**
    - Each run trains at its arm's original training pin and config, via `run_experiment.py --seed N`, device as originally: Arm 2 gnn on L40S, everything else CPU with 6 threads.
      - Arm 3 twin: a config-only commit on Arm 3's pin `32f1c6cd`.
    - Epoch cap 100, `-final.pt` served, W&B for every run, `tests/test_trainer_determinism.py` at each pin before training, and every `.pt` travels with its `.contract.json`.
    - New md5 files: `experiments/uncut_<arm>_ext_ckpt_md5.json`.
  - **Gate.**
    - The same 12 topologies 19537–19548 × g0–g3 × moderate / heavy, the same SELECT, split and slate, and the 869828 bar.
    - `_k100` and `_kr` cells, at each kind's frozen-w pin: Arm 1b W2b `59798c41`, Arm 3 `1847a484`, Arm 2 / K0 W-A2k `60fee04d`.
    - The frozen w is per kind and is reused for the new seeds.
    - The Arm 3 twin needs its own w: a venue series (exclusive pair, H3 drift rule 2 %) and a w-only pin on `1847a484`.
  - **Read.**
    - The existing reader gains `--seeds` (default `1,2`). B3 check: every filed read reproduces byte-identically through the changed reader.
    - Each extension test is the reader's statistic exactly:
      - cell = mean over the new seeds;
      - topology = median over the 4 windows of the paired %;
      - exact two-sided Wilcoxon over the 12 topologies;
      - frozen and co-tenancy verdicts, combined = the weaker, as filed.
  - **Families (Holm within each, new seeds only).**
    - **E1 (a):** Arm 3 gnn vs Arm 1b gnn, 2 rungs × 2 statistics, k = 4.
    - **E2 (b):** gnn vs twin for Arm 3 and Arm 1b, k = 8.
    - **E3 (c):** heavy whole and heavy after-360 vs `cd_ib0` for Arm 3, Arm 1b and K0, k = 6.
    - **E4 (d):** Arm 2 gnn vs K0 gnn at heavy (2 tests); with Arm 1 admitted, also K0 gnn vs Arm 1 gnn at heavy, k = 4 (else k = 2).
  - **Labels per test.** The claimed direction is "first arm faster", except E4's Arm 2 − K0, where K0 is the faster.
    - **CLAIM:** Holm p < .05 and median in the claimed direction.
    - **REVERSED:** Holm p < .05, median against the claimed direction.
    - **CLEAN-NEGATIVE:** neither of the above, and the sign-test 95 % interval for the median over the 12 topologies, [x(3), x(10)] (coverage 96.1 %), excludes a claimed-direction effect of 1 % or more. 1 % is the reader's per-seed line, below the 2 % WIN line, and is chosen post-look.
    - **UNRESOLVED:** otherwise.
    - A rung's finding is a claim (or clean negative) only if both of its statistics are, under the combined verdict.
  - **Cost (estimate).**
    - Phase 1: 32 runs × about 12 h × 12 CPUs ≈ 4.6 k CPU-h, one wave of about 14 h on 8 nodes.
    - Phase 2:
      - K0 gnn: 6 × about 66 h ≈ 4.8 k CPU-h at 12 CPUs each;
      - Arm 2 gnn: 6 × about 72 L40S-h ≈ 430 GPU-h;
      - (Arm 1 gnn: 6 × about 16 h ≈ 1.1 k CPU-h);
      - wall about 3 days.
    - Cells: about 9,600 (both charges) ≈ 1.3 k core-h, about 3 h of wall per phase on 8 nodes.
    - Arm 3 twin calibration: about 15 min on 2 nodes.

- 2026-10-17 — **Amendment to the in-flight bar registration below, before any cell (the expert's ruling): a second, COMPLETE variant behind a new default-off flag; the decision rule applies to whichever variant is faster.** (S4.) Because `HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT` adds only the one task a platform is serving (the compute-lock holder) and not the tasks waiting on the lock, a null result from it alone could not clear the bar of the blindness. **New flag** `HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT_FULL` (0/1, default 0 = the registered rule byte-identical; 1 together with `HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT` fails loud): `_pg_choose` adds `live_audit.inflight_full_seconds(platform)` to each candidate's drain, the remaining service of every task in `platform.inflight` (the set `scaleout.platform_in_flight` counts besides the queue), by stage, with the code's own estimates and no invented constant: a task that holds or has held the compute lock owes `compute_start_time + execution + output - now` (the output is `infrastructure._output_estimate_seconds`), one whose input stage is over and waits for the lock owes execution + output, one still in its input stage owes execution + `_approx_comm` (input and output at the storage rate, its progress unknown, so charged whole); execution is the table value on the candidate's platform type, as in the queue walk; counters `pg_inflight_full_charged` / `pg_inflight_full_seconds`. Gate kind `cd_ib0_inflightfull` (new, `R1A_DIAG`): `peer_greedy_network_cd` + `HEROSIM_PG_BATCH_ALPHA=0` + `HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT_FULL=1`; the cell check fails if the flag is not served or `pg_inflight_full_charged` is 0, and every other arm fails if it charged the full drain. Tests: a platform with an empty queue and three lock waiters costs more than an idle one (9.0 s vs 0), the stages price as stated, the gate knows the kind. Gate-listing proof against `da4a4d4f` with the new kinds and `heft` removed: the lists, the default grid (2,496 cells) and every listing identical except the allowed-arms text inside three fail-loud messages. **Amended decision rule.** SCIENTIST's rule below applies word for word to WHICHEVER of the two variants (`cd_ib0_inflight`, `cd_ib0_inflightfull`) is faster than `cd_ib0_k100` at moderate load by the median of the WHOLE-RUN statistic; that variant is "the in-flight bar" of the rule; the other is reported as a row. **w:** `cd_ib0`'s frozen w, unchanged, for both variants (the w-only commit gives `cd_ib0_inflightfull` the same key); the lookup cost of either variant is not recalibrated, and the full variant's per-decision cost (a walk of `platform.inflight` per candidate platform, memoised per batch) is larger than the one-task lookup's: disclosed. **Smoke:** both variants on 19537 g0 moderate against a `cd_ib0_k100` control; the control must equal the 869828 cell byte for byte; both variants must show the flag in the result file, non-zero counters and plan differences from the control, before 2 x 96 cells run. It is a validity check of the bar, not a new heuristic arm: neither variant can become a hand-lever result beyond what the decision rule says.
- 2026-10-17 — **Registration, before any cell: the in-flight sensitivity of the hand bar (descriptive; no family, no verdict change).** (S4; the expert's request after SCIENTIST's point on the load-signal audit.) **Question.** Is `cd_ib0`'s margin partly a load-signal handicap? Under `HEROSIM_REPLICA_RELEASE=1` its drain term walks `platform.queue.items` only (`live_audit.py:94`), and the in-flight term is off by default (`HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT` 0, `peer_greedy_network/scheduler.py:87`, `:329`; `_pg_running` returns 0 without an observation model, `:443-444`). **What the flag does (read from the code, to be proven by the smoke):** with `HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT=1`, `_pg_choose` adds `live_audit.inflight_remaining_seconds(platform)` to each candidate's drain, i.e. `inflight_service_end - now` of the ONE task the platform is serving now (the holder of the compute lock), counted in `pg_inflight_charged` / `pg_inflight_seconds`. It does NOT count the tasks waiting on the compute lock: it removes part of the blindness, not all of it, and it needs no observation model (`_pg_running` is the sparse_obs channel and stays 0). **Arm.** Gate kind `cd_ib0_inflight` (new, `R1A_DIAG`, outside the default grid): `peer_greedy_network_cd` with `HEROSIM_PG_BATCH_ALPHA=0` (the ib0 surrogate, as `cd_ib0`) and `HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT=1`; its instrument checks are `cd_inflight`'s (flag served and `pg_inflight_charged` > 0 in every cell). It is the only change: a gate-kind commit (4 lines) with a test, plus a w-only commit that gives it `cd_ib0`'s frozen w, unchanged (moderate 0.0116, heavy 0.01098 ms per plan, scalar). **Disclosure: the extra in-flight lookup has its own wall cost, which is not recalibrated; w is `cd_ib0`'s.** **Cells.** `cd_ib0_inflight_k100`, seed 0, the 12 gate topologies 19537-19548 x g0-g3 x moderate / heavy (96 cells), at the bar's recipe: the host-block gate on reference-physics (S5's check that `60fee04d` reproduces the 869828 bar bit-exactly is the expert's basis; here the control below checks it on this pin), CPU-amd SMT off. The bar `cd_ib0_k100` (869828) is the comparator. **Smoke, before the 96 cells and reported to the expert first:** one cell (19537 g0 moderate) as `cd_ib0_k100` (control) and as `cd_ib0_inflight_k100`: the control's `.tasks.npy` / `.tasks_bsz.npy` / `.tasks_lock.npy` must equal the 869828 cell's byte for byte (else this pin does not reproduce the bar and the row stops), and the inflight cell must show the flag in its result file (`env.HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT` = 1, `pg_inflight_charged` > 0) AND different plans (per-task completion times and the placement-dependent counters differ from the control's on that cell), not just a log line. **Read (frozen reader statistic, 557d84bf; per topology the median over windows of % (arm - bar)/bar; median over 12; W/L; exact Wilcoxon, unadjusted; descriptive):** (i) `cd_ib0_inflight_k100` vs `cd_ib0_k100`, moderate and heavy, whole and after-360; (ii) each filed learned arm vs `cd_ib0_inflight_k100` (Arm 1 gnn and MLP, Arm 1b, Arm 3, K0 and Arm 2, their filed `_k100` cells), same cells. **Decision rule (SCIENTIST's, accepted by the expert; word for word):** "1. Primary contrast (descriptive): the in-flight bar vs cd_ib0_k100 at moderate load, both statistics (whole, after-360), paired per topology, median over 12. 2. If the in-flight bar is NOT faster than the bar by >= 1 pp (median) on either moderate statistic, the paper keeps its comparison and reports the measured change. 3. If it is faster by >= 1 pp on either, the in-flight bar becomes the paper's reference hand rule. The GNN is then reported against it: Wilcoxon over 12 topologies, Holm over the 2 moderate tests, labelled as a post-registration bar change. The old bar becomes a row. 4. The heavy cells are read under the same rule, for Limitations only. 5. w: cd_ib0's frozen w(n), unchanged and stated. The smoke must show that the flag changes PLANS, i.e. plan diffs on at least one cell, not just a log line." **Checked before any cell (the expert's item 4): did sparse_obs stage 1 (DRAIN-NO-VOI) include in-flight work under replica release in this seat?** No. The seat is the same (`sparse_obs_s0_v1.md:70`: 160 clients x 24 servers, replica release, KPA). Stage 1 degraded the drain to queue counts times a service estimate against the exact walk of `queue.items` (`:30`, `:52`, `:173`: +0.73 % moderate / +0.34 % heavy); both arms read the queue only. The in-flight row (`:31`) was ruled NOT degraded (`:84`: the reference A0 `cd_exactS_ib0` does not read `HEROSIM_PG_INFLIGHT`), the running term was 0 for every arm (`:92`) and the in-flight book was 0 (`:172`). So DRAIN-NO-VOI measured the value of exact versus approximate QUEUE information, with the tasks waiting on the compute lock invisible to both; it says nothing about adding in-flight or lock-waiter work. Gate-listing proof (`7e92557f`) with the new kind removed, at `da4a4d4f` vs this commit: R1A_ARMS, RA_KINDS, RA_MP_OFF, R1A_NOSPLIT, the default grid (2,496 cells) and every listing identical except the allowed-arms text inside three fail-loud messages.
- 2026-10-17 — **Reference baselines: the reactive row was a bug in the baseline's load signal; fixed and rerun; HEFT w ruled (node B only); HEFT cells read (DESCRIPTIVE).** (S4; the expert's diagnosis and rulings.) **Reactive bug.** Under `HEROSIM_REPLICA_RELEASE=1` a task leaves `platform.queue` when its I/O starts and then waits on `compute_lock` (`infrastructure.py:1860`), but `knative_network` ranked replicas by `len(platform.queue.items)` (`knative_network/scheduler.py:219`), so every replica looked empty and the id tie-break herded the traffic onto the lowest-id replica. **This is the defect already filed in `docs/gates/gate-tools.md` (2026-10-11, "Under replica release, the Knative arm's least-connected key is blind": the key read 0 on 80-84 % of placements and the lowest id won 99.8 % of ties); the first reactive read here repeated it.** (`queue_share` is not a pure queue measure either: gate-tools 2026-10-08, third point, `74326976`.) **Fix** `eedde810` (`rp/uncut-baselines`): a subclass `KnativeInFlightScheduler` of `KnativeScheduler`, registered for the `knative_network` policy only (`simulation.py`, key `kn_network_kn_network`); under replica release it ranks by `scaleout.platform_in_flight` (queue + virtual backlog + admitted + in-flight, the concurrency the KPA counts; the queue is included, so it is not added twice), without release by the queue as before; id tie-break kept. Every other policy built on `KnativeScheduler` (`knative_network_batch`, `offload_network`, `local_first_network`, `roundrobin_network`, the ECT arms, `peer_greedy_*`) keeps the base. `tests/test_knative_inflight.py` (4): two replicas, the lower-id one with an empty queue and five lock waiters, the fix picks the other and the base class still picks the first; without release the key is the queue; ties and queued tasks. Gate-listing proof (`uncut_gate_listing_proof.py`, `7e92557f`) at `2b4617c0` vs this commit: byte-identical, md5 `5f8f4e93f628b1cf6697a46facd10e96`. **Audit of the other arms for the same blindness (read only, nothing changed; the filed reads depend on them):** the hand rules' drain term `platform_queue_drain_seconds` walks `platform.queue.items` only (`live_audit.py:94`, plus the virtual backlog at `:72`; used by `_pg_drain`, `peer_greedy_network/scheduler.py:438-441`), and `inflight_remaining_seconds` (`live_audit.py:138`) is only added under the default-off `pg_inflight`; the `scheduling_cost.py:271` queue term uses `len(platform.queue.items)`; the GNN queue features come from `state_capture.py:69,130` (`platform.queue_length()`, queue plus virtual backlog), `gnn/orchestrator.py:193` and `live_audit.py:199,276`, all queue-only; `random` has no load signal (`random/scheduler.py:138`); `KnativeScheduler` itself and the arms inheriting its `placement` (`knative_network_batch`, `offload_network`, `local_first_network`) keep the queue-only key (`knative_network/scheduler.py`, `_connections`), as do `knative/scheduler.py:52`, `tabular/single_scheduler.py:172` and `herocache_network*/scheduler.py:282,326`; the KPA autoscaler already counts in-flight (`placement/autoscaler.py:256`, `scaleout.py:108`), and its other paths use the queue (`autoscaler.py:483,546,552,647,723`). Whether the queue-only signal matters to the bar and the learned arms is a separate question (the standing finding: the drain term's queue coefficient is about 0 and zeroing it live is neutral); none of it is changed here. **Rerun** (job 883585 at `eedde810`, os-cpu-slurm-10, exclusive, SMT off, EPYC 9335, 7:13, 96/96 cells, 0 failed). Reactive vs `cd_ib0_k100`, median over 12 topologies (all 0/12 ahead, p .00049), OLD (blind key) -> FIXED: moderate whole +9,236.7 -> **+173.2 %**; moderate after-360 +12,019.0 -> **+225.3 %**; heavy whole +18,849.5 -> **+232.3 %**; heavy after-360 +36,343.8 -> **+459.2 %**; median end of run minus last arrival 6,647 s -> 100 s. The fixed reactive row lands within a few percent of random's (+175.9 / +225.9 / +248.2 / +466.2 %). Files `baselines/baselines_read_2.{json,txt}` md5 `3d83b2ac...` / `e18ef53b...`. **HEFT w.** Series 883437 (void: no host block; its drift STOP on node A, heavy +3.24 %, stays filed) and series 883562 at `a743cc35` (os-cpu-slurm-9 = A, 10 = B, exclusive, SMT off, EPYC 9335 on all six step starts, 4:02): **the drift stop fired again on node A** (cd_ib0 heavy -2.28 %, moderate -0.23 %), node B passed both series (heavy +0.98 / +0.46 %, moderate -0.21 / -0.74 %). Ruling (a): **w from node B only; this deviates from the filed pooling of A and B.** w = 0.003035 (moderate) / 0.002807 (heavy) ms per metered evaluation, scalar (the served-batch buckets are 0.78-1.00 of the le10 rate); pooled A+B would be 0.003060 / 0.002820; node A alone 0.003084 / 0.002833 (`baselines/heft_freeze_w_series5.json`). Two w-only commits on `eedde810`: `04a29eb1` (node B only, the ruled one, on `rp/uncut-baselines`) and `25412f2a` (pooled, sensitivity, on `rp/uncut-baselines-wpooled`); each changes only `decision_cost_w.json`. **HEFT cells** (`heft_k100`, seed 0, 96 cells each, 0 failed, jobs 883588 on slurm-9 at `04a29eb1` and 883589 on slurm-10 at `25412f2a`, 7:47 / 7:40). vs `cd_ib0_k100`, median over 12, all 0/12 ahead, p .00049, node-B w: moderate whole **+16.43 %**, moderate after-360 **+22.18 %**, heavy whole **+18.49 %**, heavy after-360 **+34.13 %** (median end of run minus last arrival 72 s); pooled w: +16.58 / +21.71 / +18.76 / +33.00 %. The sign and W/L do not depend on the w; the medians differ by at most 1.1 pp, but single topologies move up to 2.4 / 1.8 / 4.7 / 10.3 pp between the two w's (a 0.8 % change in w; the cells are not smooth in w) (`baselines/heft_w_sensitivity.txt`). HEFT is slower than the full-cost greedy `cd` (+5.5 / +7.8 / +4.6 / +9.3 %) as well as the bar, as expected of a one-pass rule that ignores in-batch coordination (it is the batched scorer in rank order); its metered evaluations are about 5.5x the batched greedy's (ranking included). Files `baselines/baselines_read_2.{json,txt}`, `heft_w_sensitivity.txt`.

They are random placement's collapse, not a bug, and are counted, not rerun; an arm's cell needs both seeds, so random has 41 of 48 cells at moderate and 47 of 48 at heavy. **Definitions.** `reactive` = `knative_network` (`src/policy/knative_network/scheduler.py:182-219`): per arrival, among the network-reachable replicas it takes the initialized ones if any and picks the one with the shortest queue (`len(platform.queue.items)`), ties by node id then platform id; replicas are sized by the Knative-style KPA autoscaler (`HEROSIM_SCALEOUT=kpa`, `knative_network/autoscaler.py:49`). - 2026-10-17 — **Amendment, filed before any cell: three descriptive reference baselines (random, Knative-style reactive, HEFT-style list scheduler) on the final live table** (the user's request, relayed by the expert: "We need at least random, a Knative-style reactive scheduler and a HEFT-style list scheduler, since we cite HEFT but don't compare against it."; S4). **They are reference rows. They are not a new F1 family, join no Holm family, take no program alpha, carry no WIN/LOSS label and change no filed verdict.** **Arms.** (1) `random`: the gate kind `random` (`random_network`, a uniformly random eligible replica per arrival), existing, seeds 1 and 2. (2) `reactive`: the gate kind `reactive` (`knative_network`, the Knative-style reactive scheduler), existing, seed 0. (3) `heft`: NEW gate kind `heft` (`src/policy/heft_network/scheduler.py`, policy `heft_network_batch`, registered in `simulation.py`, `model.py` and `executesimulation.py`; `RULE_POLICY['heft']`, in `R1A_DIAG` so the default grid is untouched), seed 0. **HEFT-style, as built.** A subclass of the registered batched greedy (`peer_greedy_network_batch`), served on the same stack (peer-group batching, masked_topo path, no model). Phase 1: upward rank over the batch, `rank_u(i) = mean_exec(i) + max_j (mean_comm(i, j) + rank_u(j))` with `mean_exec` the mean of the execution-time table over the task's candidate platforms, successors = batch tasks with a larger id that are i's exchange partner or have i in their dependencies (ids follow the workload's topological order), `mean_comm` the mean transfer time plus link latency of the pair's exchange payload over at most 8 sender nodes x 8 receiver platforms (the first by node name). Phase 2: tasks placed in decreasing rank (ties by id), each on the eligible replica of least finish time, which is the registered scorer S unchanged (queue drain including the service already committed in this batch, + cold start + execution + ingress latency + exchange to the partners already placed). **That scorer is what `batched` already uses, so HEFT differs from `batched` in the placement ORDER only**; no refine pass, no search, no learned part. Disclosed limits: the ranking is an estimate over a bounded node sample; a group with no exchange partners and no dependencies is ordered by mean execution time (longest first). **Decision time.** `random` and `reactive` meter no plan evaluations, so no decision time is charged to them (they run as the plain kinds, no k suffix). `heft` is charged as the hand rules are: k = 1 (`_k100`) x its frozen w in ms per metered evaluation, where every candidate score and every transfer-time evaluation of the ranking is one evaluation; w is measured on the exclusive SMT-off EPYC 9335 pair os-cpu-slurm-9 / 10 in the hand/learned calibration recipe (19461 g0, both rungs, k = 0, seed 0, PAR 1, 1 thread), as a series H3pre -> heft -> H3end with the 2 % drift stop, and enters a w-only commit on the code commit; scalar w unless a served-batch bucket exceeds 1.5x the le10 rate, then by bucket (the rule of the w(n) freeze). **Cells.** The same 12 gate topologies 19537-19548 x windows g0-g3 x moderate / heavy as the bar `cd_ib0_k100` (the 869828 cells at `d6ae87c2`, run on os-cpu-slurm-6 with SMT on): 96 cells for `reactive`, 96 for `heft`, 192 for `random` (2 seeds). The new cells run on CPU-amd with SMT off (the bar's venue is not matched; the simulated latency does not depend on the host except through w, which is frozen for `heft` and absent for the other two; the proofs of sim-identity of hand cells across pins stand). **Statistic:** per topology the median over windows of 100 (arm - bar) / bar with the arm's cell mean the mean over its seeds, whole run and after 360 s, then the median over topologies, the mean, and wins (topologies ahead of the bar) of 12, computed with the frozen reader's `contrast` (557d84bf), as the interim reads do. **Smoke on the study's own artifacts, before the cells:** `heft` and `batched` on topology 19461 g0 (the calibration topology), both rungs, k = 0: the cells complete, the batch counters are non-zero, the placement differs from `batched`'s on at least one batch (else the ranking is inert) and the plan-eval count per decision is reported. **Proofs with this commit:** unit tests (`tests/test_heft_network.py`, 8: rank math, order, no refine, metering, registration, gate kind), gate/stage/hygiene suites passing, and the gate-listing proof (`uncut_gate_listing_proof.py`, `7e92557f`) with the new kind removed, at `16dc0b8d` and at this commit: R1A_ARMS, RA_KINDS, RA_MP_OFF and R1A_NOSPLIT identical, the default grid (2,496 cells) and every existing listing identical; the only differing text is the list of allowed arms inside three fail-loud error messages, which now names `heft`. The 57 `accel_replica_v1` test ids are not used. B6 does not apply (no learned arm, no label).

- 2026-10-17 — **Final-read linearity check for Arm 1 and Arm 1b (registered check (2)): no queueing amplification; per-cell linearity cannot be resolved because per-cell scatter is several times larger than the predicted shift** (S6). Descriptive. It changes no verdict. The lineage stays open while the user decides on the next arm.
  - **Method.** For each frozen-w cell (`_k100`) and its co-tenancy twin (`_kr`, k 1.468), same arm, seed, topology, window and rung:
    - observed = 100 (mean_kr − mean_k100) / mean_k100 (whole-run averageElapsedTime);
    - predicted = (k − 1) × F_L = 0.468 × F_L, with F_L the `_k100` cell's felt charge share at the pin's frozen w. F_L comes from `uncut_interim_share.cell` (tool 5c8c959e, md5 f0229b97, default one-plan-per-task path).

    - The registered failure is a `_kr` − frozen-w difference many times larger than predicted, meaning queueing amplifies the charge. It does not occur: no arm's median or mean difference exceeds about 2.5× its prediction, and most are at or below it.

Only 5–12 % of cells fall within 0.5–2× of their prediction, and the per-cell sign is often negative. A 1.468× charge changes the simulated trajectory, so the paired cells diverge. Linearity at the cell level is therefore not resolvable on these cells.

- 2026-10-15 — **Expected move-regret lever, step 2 timing and a ruling made AFTER it: unscored candidates take d = 10.** (S4; the expert's ruling (a); the stop was the expert's, added before the timing.) Timing job 880603 (pin `495a7c40`, 1 thread, 3 min): the ILS-labelled Arm 1 train set has 3,118 groups (126 <= 10, 1,960 in 11-50, 798 in 51-100, 234 in 101-256); 50 groups per bucket, mean wall 0.30 / 0.33 / 0.55 / 1.73 s per group; **projection for the full label 0.42 CPU-h** (limit 3). The two counted cases: **undecided task set to 0: 0.0 % of tasks in every bucket. Unscored candidate set to 10: 7.10 % of the non-label candidates of decided tasks (the strict denominator) and 6.31 % of all candidates**; by bucket (strict) 10.5 % (<= 10), 11.8 % (11-50), 3.3 % (51-100), 8.0 % (101-256). Over the expert's 5 % line, so the full label stopped for a ruling.
  - **Ruling (option a), made after the timing.** d = 10 for every candidate the labeller's engine never scored. (b), d = 0 on them, would make the regret gradient pull probability toward unmeasured candidates; (c), scoring them, is engine work for probability mass the model barely uses. **These candidates lie outside the labeller's engine set, so d = 10 means "never measured", not "measured costly".** The table stays a function of S and of the engine's candidate set.
  - **D0 fact, as my inference.** In D0 the gnn_eng checkpoints' teacher-forced misses in 51-256 landed on an unscored candidate in 9 of 2,310 cases (0.4 %). So the model puts little probability there and d = 10 probably moves the loss little. That is an inference from where the misses fell, not a measurement of the mass; the diagnostic below measures it.
  - **Diagnostic (added with this ruling).** The table and a mask-only val file carry a per-candidate flag for the never-scored ones. The trainer logs, per epoch, `train/unscored_mass` and `val/unscored_mass`: the model's mean probability on those candidates over the label plan's choice steps (teacher-forced state; train is in train mode, dropout on). Train reads the move-regret table's flags; val reads `NEAR_RTT_UNSCORED_MASK_VAL_PATH`, a mask-only file for the ILS-labelled val groups (`merge --which val`: no move cost, the trainer refuses a val file that has one, and any key that is not a val id), so val groups still carry no regret and nothing trains on them. A group without a mask adds nothing; the loss, its gradient and Arm 1b at lambda = 0 are unchanged (the diagnostic reads the log-probs after the loss is built; a test checks the loss is equal with and without it).
  - **Stage 1 addition (descriptive; the filed thresholds do not change).** For the lambda runs against Arm 1b at the same seed, stage 1 also reports the model's mean probability on never-scored candidates (teacher-forced, val) and the free-decode pick rate of never-scored candidates on val, from the same D0-style table on the val groups.
  - **Tests and disclosure.** `tests/test_batched_tf.py` (the mass equals the sequential walk's label-state probability on the masked candidates, changes nothing else, is a no-op without a mask; the trainer text checks) and `tests/test_move_regret_label.py` (the flags): with the lambda tests, job on a datalab worktree at `495a7c40` with the five changed files tar-overlaid (md5 prefixes in the commit: `batched_tf.py`, `train_near_rtt.py`, both test files, `uncut_move_regret_label.py`; equal on both sides), `test_batched_tf.py` + `test_trainer_determinism.py` + `test_move_regret_label.py`: 66 passed, 12 skipped (CUDA). - 2026-10-15 — **Expected move-regret lever, step 2: the labelling script and its 200-group timing (code and Record; the timing job runs after this is merged).** (S4; the expert's rulings: the table is a sidecar pickle keyed by dataset_id and `graphs.pkl` stays untouched, so step 3 (the cache rebuild) is dropped; the trainer logs the table coverage to W&B and fails loud on a stray key.) `scripts_cosim/uncut_move_regret_label.py` computes, for every ILS-labelled group (placements.jsonl header `label_source == "ils"`) of the Arm 1 TRAIN split (`experiments/uncut_arm1_split.json` `train`, 9,745 groups), D_c(t) = S(label with task t moved to c) - S(label) with the labeller's own S (ib0, `_pg_plan_cost`) through the same engine pass as D0, and stores d = min(D / (S(label)/n), 10) per candidate in the graph's own candidate order (0 on the label's candidate). Per group it asserts that the id is not one of the 220 test placeholders of the Arm 1 split nor one of the 57 `accel_replica_v1` test ids, and that its topology (`generation_provenance.json` seed) is not 16226, 16251-16270 or 19537-19548. Text-level tests in `tests/test_batched_tf.py` and the table-row tests in `tests/test_move_regret_label.py` cover them.
  - **Cost rule (the expert's).** Time on 200 groups first (4 buckets x 50, or all if fewer); run the full label only if the projection is <= 3 CPU-h; otherwise report to the expert. The full label is an 8-task array (`uncut_lever_label.sbatch`, 1 CPU each, 64 GB for the cache load) and a merge job. Checks: the four Arm 1b `-final.pt` md5s re-checked; the test-id / banned-topology assertion (57 `accel_replica_v1` test ids; topologies 16226, 16251-16270, 19537-19548) passed on all 334 groups; the teacher-forced hit count equals S5's `decode_group` `tf_hit` for every group x checkpoint (334 x 2 gnn, 334 x 2 twin); the label is a strict local optimum of the labeller's S under single-task moves (0 improving moves of 101,000).
  - **Rule applied.** NO-SIGNAL needs the top 20 % of misses to carry < 50 % of cost in BOTH 51-100 and 101-256 (cost = d clipped at 10, the two gnn_eng checkpoints pooled within the bucket). 51-100: **C20 = 55.8 %** [48.6, 65.1] on 1,403 scored misses; 101-256: **C20 = 48.8 %** [42.0, 57.5] on 898. Scored share 99.4 % and 100.0 % (the 80 % guard is met in both). 51-100 is above 50 %, so the rule does not fire: **SIGNAL-PRESENT**. Both intervals span 50 %; the verdict is the point-estimate rule as filed.
  - **What the misses look like (gnn_eng s1 + s2 pooled).** Miss rate 10.5 % (51-100) and 14.3 % (101-256) of choice steps (11-50: 8.6 %). Median miss cost d = 1.42 / 2.06 mean-per-task costs; 56.0 / 62.4 % of misses cost at least one unit; 22.3 / 25.7 % hit the clip at 10. Share of choice steps whose every alternative costs at least one unit: 58.8 / 52.7 %. Per checkpoint: s1 56.2 / 49.5 %, s2 55.5 / 48.2 %. Twin (descriptive): 56.8 / 49.8 %. 11-50 (descriptive): 47.7 %.
  - **Sensitivity to the clip (post hoc, descriptive, not part of the rule).** On the same misses C20 is 0.885 (51-100) and 0.817 (101-256) with no clip, and 0.379 / 0.340 with the clip at 3: the cost of a miss is extremely heavy-tailed, and what C20 says depends on where the clip sits. The registered clip is 10.
  - **Read.** Misses are not cheap near-ties: more than half cost a full mean per-task cost or more, and the costliest fifth carries about half (clip 10) or most (no clip) of the total. Cross-entropy gives all of them the same gradient. That is the condition under which a regret term can differ from CE; it is not evidence that it helps. The concentration under the registered clip is moderate (a uniform cost would give 20 %), and only a minority of the cost sits in a few misses, so a modest effect would not be a surprise. Nothing else is approved: **training waits for the expert's ruling on these numbers** (the 8-run plan, queue <= 44, not taking CPU-amd nodes from the Saturday 02:00 calibration 871538, goes with this result).
  - **Files.** `d0/d0.json` (md5 `6fc30e4f…`), `d0/d0.md` (md5 `7a735250…`). Scratch under `/share/nikola.lukic/uncut_groups/d0/` and the pin worktree are deleted after this entry.

- 2026-10-15 — **Expected move-regret lever: REGISTERED-DESIGN, with D0 (a no-training diagnostic) approved to run and nothing else** (S4's design note, the expert's ruling "(c) accepted, only D0 now"). **Post-interim, no program alpha; this lever is conceived after interim reads and is at best WIN-ARM-ONLY (B6).** Reads its authors had seen when it was designed: Arm 1b look 1 (twin) and look 2 (gnn) in `interim_looks.md`; the Arm 1b replay diagnostic (S5; result and the two relabels); W3 (`3592e070`); the W3 label-prefix control (`7197ad4c`); the Arm 2 decision-time profile at epoch 47 (S6); the serve-fast profile. Not read: look 3 (K0 twin, sealed). **Nearest prior objective changes: `drainable_objective_v1` (a shaped label) and `objective_pivot_v1` (horizon labels). Neither is this loss.** Related closures it touches: `docs/hard-stops.md` 2026-09-25 "Relabelling or re-serving the burst-seat GNN to reach the CD greedy" (imitation of search plans on the old architecture; the uncut models carry committed load, so it is not a bar, but it argues against imitation alone), `rollout_imitation_v1` (RULE-FASTER-LIVE), `cost_to_go_v1` (NO-WIN), and the `oracle_terms` probe (S is nearly additive; a residual model gave no plan gain).
  - **Why this lever.** The W3 control put the room in the model's choices after the prefix even with the label's prefix (34.6 % vs 37.0 %; D = +2.4 pp [-3.8, 8.8], NO-EXPOSURE-ROOM), so self-prefix training is dropped. OBJECTIVE (gnn s2) fired: per-task match rose +3.3 pp from the best-val epoch to epoch 100 with replayed regret vs hand unchanged. The loss in Arm 1/1b is a one-hot cross-entropy on the label at each teacher-forced step, which counts a near-tie miss and a plan-wrecking miss alike. Candidates not taken: (a) distilling ILS completions (Arm 1b's objective when the prefix is the label; the self-prefix idea otherwise); (b) a completion-stage re-score pass (needs a scorer trained on all-others-committed states and about triples the decode; a possible second step).
  - **The lever (registered).** At each teacher-forced step of an ILS-labelled group (prefix = the label's placements, as now), with p the model's distribution over the step's candidates: L = CE(label) + lambda x sum_c p_c d_c. Move cost D_c = S(label with task t moved to c) - S(label), S the labeller's own plan cost (`_pg_plan_cost`, ib0; the single-task neighbours the ILS' ICM pass evaluates); d_c = D_c / (S(label) / n), clipped to [0, 10]. **lambda in {0.5, 2.0}, nothing else swept.** lambda = 0 is Arm 1b exactly (a bit-identity test at one seed in the style of `tests/test_trainer_determinism.py`, before any run). The term applies only to ILS-labelled groups; hand-fallback groups (label = the hand plan; S and the replay disagree on 16 / 22 / 34 % of them) keep the one-hot CE. Same architecture and decode, so the decision time moves only through the model (w guard 1.5x against Arm 1 as filed). A loss flag in the batched trainer plus an `experiments/` config; no forked trainer; epoch cap 100; every run to W&B (project `gnn-uncut-arm1`, run names `uncut-arm3-gnn-eng-l{0p5,2p0}-seed{1,2}`, tags `lever-regret`).
  - **D0 (approved; the only thing that runs).** No training. Existing Arm 1b `-final.pt` checkpoints (gnn_eng seeds 1 and 2: `f8b8be77761a020b1179f0853ae2f0a2`, `95f391d1a1638f456cd3e091f01f10c6`; twin_eng seeds 1 and 2 descriptive: `2c22db5270c05c3f6a115b6a47fdf903`, `9e337dd6a9c8659e082e9292102a8fe1`; md5 file `experiments/uncut_arm1b_ckpt_md5.json`, re-checked by the job) on the ILS-labelled val groups, all buckets (334: 12 / 197 / 104 / 21 in <= 10 / 11-50 / 51-100 / 101-256; `d0_draw.json`, md5 `4aac07d1f4df579421d4ea7cfa10549d`); development data (val groups, including the 257 of 51-256 spent by W3 and W3c, are never confirmatory). For every choice step the teacher-forced argmax (S5's teacher-forced loop; the hit count is asserted equal to S5's `decode_group` `tf_hit` for every group x checkpoint) is compared with the label; each MISS gets its move cost d (the engine's own S on the label plan, one-task moves; a miss whose candidate the engine never scored is counted as unscored, not costed). Reported per bucket, for the two gnn checkpoints pooled (primary) and per checkpoint, the twin beside them: miss rate, the distribution of d over misses (median, p90, mean, share d >= 1, share clipped), **C20 = the share of the total miss cost carried by the costliest 20 % (rounded up) of the misses**, with a group-bootstrap 95 % interval (2,000 resamples, seed 20261015), the share of choice steps whose every alternative costs d >= 1, the label's local-optimality (the share of single-task moves with negative cost), and the unscored share.

- 2026-10-15 — **W3 label-prefix control: result. NO-EXPOSURE-ROOM under the filed rule. INTERIM-DESCRIPTIVE, MYOPIC-REPLAY (offline replayed regret on the group's own tasks, not live latency); diagnostic only, no gate. Preceding looks: Arm 1b look 1, look 2, the replay diagnostic and W3 (`3592e070`); the control was specified after W3's result was read.** (S4.) Pin `d10a4ade` (scripts identical to `f9d4f813`). Jobs: points rerun 879918 (the first, 879902, aborted at its identity check on a list-vs-tuple comparison, see the entry below; its outputs were deleted), one-group smoke 879988 (not an input), ILS array 880019 (8 tasks, all COMPLETED), analysis 880389. Checks: unforced wrapper equals S5's `decode_group` on 250/250 group x checkpoint (0 failures, types normalised), all-forced equals the label plan 250/250, no forced prefix altered, 0 decode failures, 0 CAPPED runs, label replay equals the stored RTT on 125/125 (max relative error 8.6e-16); the free-decode plans are in `points.json.checks.json` (md5 `0a9fa269…`).
  - **Rule.** D = (share of points with gain >= 5 % under the model's own prefix, W3) - (the same share under the label prefix, here), paired by group / checkpoint / point; 95 % group-bootstrap interval. EXPOSURE-ROOM needs D >= 10 pp with an interval excluding 0 in 51–100 or in 101–256. 51–100 (416 points, 104 groups): D = **3.1 pp** [-3.8, 9.9]. 101–256 (84 points, 21 groups): D = **-1.2 pp** [-19.0, 16.7]. Neither bucket meets EXPOSURE-ROOM. NO-EXPOSURE-ROOM needs the upper limit of D pooled over both buckets below 10 pp: pooled (500 points, 125 groups) D = **2.4 pp** [-3.8, **8.8**], so the verdict is **NO-EXPOSURE-ROOM**. The upper limit is 1.2 pp under the line, and 101–256 alone cannot exclude a 10 pp effect (its interval is 36 pp wide); the pooled interval is dominated by 51–100.
  - **The control's own numbers (search-labelled groups, both checkpoints, both points).** Share with gain >= 5 %: label prefix **34.6 %** [28.6, 41.2], model's own prefix (W3, the same 125 groups) **37.0 %** [29.8, 44.0]; share <= -5 %: 5.2 % vs 6.0 %; mean gain 6.1 % [4.3, 7.8] vs 7.5 % [5.2, 9.7]; paired mean-gain difference 1.4 pp [-0.6, 3.7]. By bucket, control vs W3: 51–100 32.5 % vs 35.6 %, 101–256 45.2 % vs 44.0 %. By prefix point, control vs W3 (51–100): t = floor(0.25 n) 43.8 % vs 46.2 %, t = floor(0.5 n) 21.2 % vs 25.0 %. In 171 of 500 control points the ILS found nothing better than the model's completion. ILS's own S-vs-replay disagreement on the control runs: 329 points S-better, 62 (19 %) replay worse (51–100: 257 / 51; 101–256: 72 / 11). The full per-cell table is `w3c/w3c_analysis.md`.
  - **Read.** Freezing the LABEL's prefix (a search optimum there) and letting the model complete the rest leaves as much room for the ILS as freezing the model's own prefix: the room W3 found is in the model's completion (the free decode of the remaining tasks leaves a gap an ILS can close), not in the damage done by the model's own earlier errors. Under the filed rule the self-prefix training arm is dropped. This does not say the model's free decode is good: it says that a prefix taken from the label does not remove the gap, so training the model on its own prefixes is not what that gap points at. Limits as filed: search-labelled groups only (the hand-fallback groups did not run, the verdict being clear under the rule, not UNCLEAR); one engine seed; the first tied label plan; the control keeps t.

- 2026-10-15 — **W3 label-prefix control: spec filed before any job runs** (S4; the user's approval relayed by the expert, who also ruled that it runs now rather than after Arm 2). **INTERIM-DESCRIPTIVE, MYOPIC-REPLAY (offline replayed regret on the group's own tasks at t = 0, not live latency); diagnostic only, no gate, relabels nothing; post-interim, no program alpha.** Preceding looks: Arm 1b look 1, look 2, the Arm 1b replay diagnostic, and **W3 itself (2026-10-15, `3592e070`: PASS under its filed rule, marginally, with the no-control caveat)**. This control exists because of that caveat and was specified after W3's result was read; it changes nothing in W3. Question: is the room W3 found (the ILS completion of the model's own frozen prefix beats the model's own completion) caused by the model's own prefix, or would the ILS beat the model's completion from any prefix, even one taken from the label?
  - **Pins.** Branch `rp/uncut-w3c` from reference-physics `741c3650`. `src/`, `scripts_cosim/uncut_ils.py`, `uncut_replay_diag.py` and `uncut_w3_prefix.py` are unchanged since W3's pin `a93b3489` (`git diff a93b3489 741c3650` empty for them; `uncut_w3_prefix.py` gets an additive edit in this commit: when a group's record carries `decoded_by_frac` the model plan is taken per point and stored as `model_key`; with no such field the code path is W3's). Checkpoints: the same two Arm 1b `gnn_eng` `-final.pt`, md5 `f8b8be77761a020b1179f0853ae2f0a2` and `95f391d1a1638f456cd3e091f01f10c6` (md5 file at `172268e6`), re-checked by the job. Decode at 1 torch thread on CPU; the engine seed is 0 as in W3.
  - **Groups (scope (b)).** The search-labelled val groups only: 104 in 51–100 and 21 in 101–256 (125 groups, `w3c_draw.json`, md5 `aa43a3ba62a0e145d63d6931717277c9`, a subset of W3's `w3_draw.json`), 500 points (2 checkpoints x 2 prefix points). The label prefix is a real search optimum there. The 41 + 91 hand-fallback groups, where the "label" is the hand plan, run **only if (b) is UNCLEAR** under the rule below; if they run, they run the same pipeline, ahead of any reading of them, cost about 11.6 CPU-h (W3's measured search wall on those groups), and the verdict is then re-evaluated on all 257 groups pooled with the same rule and the per-class figures beside it.
  - **Prefix points.** Identical to W3: t = floor(0.25 n) and floor(0.5 n) tasks, the first t tasks in DAG topological order (Kahn, lowest task id first).
  - **Control arm.** Prefix = the label plan's own placements for those t tasks (S5's first tied label plan, as `uncut_replay_diag` uses it). Completion by the model: the served `masked_topo` decode of each checkpoint with the first t tasks forced to their label placement and the remaining tasks free, each scored on a committed state that holds the label placements. Then the same ILS as W3 (`uncut_ils.ils` through `decision_pass`, arm `cd_ib0`) with that label prefix frozen (same freeze mechanism), start = the forced-decode plan, budget round(B0 x (n - t) / n) with B0 the group's unfrozen default. **Metric:** gain_ctrl = (R(model completion under the label prefix) - R(label prefix + ILS completion)) / R(model completion under the label prefix), R the fidelity replay of the group's own tasks.
  - **New decode logic: `force_prefix_score_fn`** (`scripts_cosim/uncut_w3_control.py`), a wrapper around the served scorer. It calls the scorer at every step (its incremental state stays in step) and, for a forced task, lifts the label candidate above the others by 1e9 over the scorer's own maximum, leaving the other scores as the model gave them; nothing in `seq_decode` is edited. Checks, all FAIL LOUD or counted: (1) with nothing forced it must equal S5's `decode_group` plan on every group x checkpoint (an inequality aborts the job); (2) with every task forced the decode must equal the label plan (counted and listed); (3) the label placement of a forced task must be one of its candidates (error); (4) after the forced decode the prefix must still hold the label placements (a point where the mask or the backtrack changed it gets no ILS run and is counted); plus W3's own checks (start equals the forced plan, prefix kept in the result, label replay equals the stored RTT at 1e-9). `tests/test_w3_forced_prefix.py` covers (1), (2) and (3) on a synthetic scorer (11 tests).
  - **Rule (unchanged from the proposal).** For each bucket, the paired per-point difference D = (share of points with gain >= 5 % under the model's own prefix, W3) - (the same share under the label prefix, this control), over the same group / checkpoint / point, with a 95 % group-bootstrap interval (2,000 resamples of groups, a group's points kept together, seed 20261015). **EXPOSURE-ROOM** if in 51–100 or in 101–256 D >= 10 pp and the interval excludes 0: the model's own prefix is what carries the room, and the self-prefix arm survives to a build decision. Otherwise **NO-EXPOSURE-ROOM** if the interval's upper limit on D pooled over both buckets is below 10 pp: the room is the completer's, not the prefix's, and the self-prefix arm is dropped. Otherwise **UNCLEAR**, which runs the hand-fallback groups as above. The 101–256 bucket has 21 groups and its interval will be wide; the NO-EXPOSURE-ROOM line is on the pooled interval for that reason.
  - **Also reported (descriptive).** The control's own shares at >= 5 % and <= -5 %, median and mean gain with group-bootstrap CIs, the paired mean-gain difference, per bucket, seed and point; ILS's own S-vs-replay disagreement on the control runs; counts of decode-failed, not-kept and CAPPED runs and the OVER2X list (flag: search wall above twice W3's fit x (n - t) / n; W3's fit `0.0072059 x n^2.0689`).
  - **Cost.** CPU only, 1 thread per run. Estimate 7.8 CPU-h (W3's measured search wall on these 125 groups: 3.67 + 4.10 CPU-h; ILS wall is linear in budget and the control uses the same budgets), plus the decode job (minutes). Hard cap 12 CPU-h for (b). One 4-CPU prep + points job, an 8-task ILS array, one analysis job; queue stays <= 44. Scratch `/share/nikola.lukic/uncut_groups/w3c/`, deleted after the result is filed; the analysis json/md and the per-group rows are kept in `docs/lineages/uncut_groups_s0_v1/w3c/`. Files: `scripts_cosim/uncut_w3_control.py`, `scripts_cosim/datalab/uncut_w3c_{prep,ils,analyze}.sbatch`.
  - **Limits stated before the data.** The control fixes where the prefix comes from, not its length (t is the same); one engine seed; the label plan is the first of the tied optima; replayed regret on the group's own tasks, not live latency; a result of EXPOSURE-ROOM licenses a build decision, not a build.

- 2026-10-15 — **W3 self-prefix pre-check: result. PASS under the filed rule, marginally. INTERIM-DESCRIPTIVE, MYOPIC-REPLAY (offline replayed regret on the group's own tasks, not live latency); diagnostic only, no gate; preceding looks: Arm 1b look 1, look 2, the Arm 1b replay diagnostic. Spec: the entry below, unchanged.** (S4.) Jobs: prep + decode + points 879153, one-group smoke 879154 (not an input), ILS array 879158 (16 tasks, all COMPLETED), analysis 879432 (a first analysis job, 879427, was cancelled before it ran because array task 3 had not finished). Pin `a93b3489`. 257 val groups, 2 checkpoints x 2 prefix points = 1,028 points; 0 decode failures, 0 CAPPED runs; label replay equals the stored RTT on 257/257 (max relative error 8.6e-16); every run's start equals the model's plan and keeps its prefix (checked in the job).
  - **Rule.** (i) gain >= 5 % on >= 30 % of the points in 51–100 or in 101–256, pooled over seeds, points and label classes; (ii) that share exceeds the share with gain <= -5 % by >= 15 pp. 51–100 (780 points, 195 groups): **31.4 %** [26.0, 37.1] vs 4.6 % [2.6, 7.1] at <= -5 %, difference **26.8 pp** [20.6, 33.1]: (i) and (ii) hold. 101–256 (248 points, 62 groups): **36.3 %** [26.2, 47.2] vs 10.5 % [4.0, 17.7], difference **25.8 pp** [12.5, 39.5]: both hold. PASS in both buckets. Read it as marginal: the lower CI limit of (i) is below 30 % in both buckets and the lower limit of (ii) is below 15 pp in 101–256; (ii) clears comfortably in 51–100.
  - **By prefix point (pooled over seeds and classes).** t = floor(0.25 n): 51–100 40.8 % [34.4, 47.4] vs 4.4 %, 101–256 41.9 % [29.8, 54.0] vs 11.3 %. t = floor(0.5 n): 51–100 **22.1 %** [16.7, 27.4] vs 4.9 %, 101–256 30.6 % [21.0, 41.9] vs 9.7 %. The 0.5 n point alone would not pass (i) in 51–100; the rule pools the points, so the verdict does not depend on it, and the gain shrinks as the prefix grows, as it must.
  - **Size of the gain.** Median gain is 0.0 % [0.0, 0.7] in 51–100 and 0.2 % [0.0, 3.1] in 101–256: in 330 of 780 and 69 of 248 points the ILS finds nothing better than the model's own completion (plan unchanged, gain exactly 0). Mean gain 6.2 % [4.7, 7.9] (51–100) and 5.1 % [1.8, 8.5] (101–256). The gain is a heavy right tail on a minority of groups, not a shift of the typical group. Seeds agree (gnn s1 / s2 share >= 5 %: 31.5 / 31.3 % in 51–100, 35.5 / 37.1 % in 101–256).
  - **By label class (the same two buckets; pooled over seeds and points).** ILS-labelled: 51–100 35.6 % [27.9, 43.0] vs 4.8 %, 101–256 (84 points, 21 groups) 44.0 % [28.6, 60.7] vs 11.9 %. Hand-fallback: 51–100 26.6 % [19.0, 34.6] vs 4.4 %, 101–256 (164 points, 41 groups) 32.3 % [20.1, 45.1] vs 9.8 %. The ILS-labelled groups, where the label is an ILS optimum, show more room than the hand-fallback groups; hand-fallback in 51–100 alone is below 30 %. The full per-bucket x class x seed x point table with median and mean gain and 95 % group-bootstrap CIs is `w3/w3_analysis.md`.
  - **ILS's own S-vs-replay disagreement on these runs.** Points where the engine's S says the completion is better: 450 in 51–100, of which **69 (15 %)** replay worse and 36 replay >= 5 % worse; 179 in 101–256, of which **53 (30 %)** replay worse and 26 >= 5 % worse. That is the noise the (ii) floor is set against; it is in the range S5 saw on hand groups (16–34 %) and is higher in the large bucket.
  - **Limits that travel with the verdict.** (1) There is no control that separates "the model's prefix is wrong" from "ILS improves any plan from a mediocre start": the metric is the gain of an ILS completion over the model's own, and the pre-check was specified without a label-prefix or random-prefix arm. PASS says completions from the model's prefixes leave room; it does not say the room is caused by the model's errors in the prefix. (2) The gain is a mean-driven tail (median about 0), so it supports a label that is occasionally much better, not a uniform correction. (3) Replayed regret on the group's own tasks at t = 0, not live latency; K1(b) is untouched. (4) One engine seed.
  - **Budget and cost.** ILS search wall 19.4 CPU-h over 1,028 runs (group wall 19.7 CPU-h; the 16-task array was allocated 39.8 CPU-h in all, with the prep job, because tasks finished unevenly); estimate was 17–18, caps 20 then 24. **OVER2X** (search wall > 2 x the fit x (n - t)/n): 133 of 1,028 runs (12.9 %, under the 15 % line), in 35 groups (30 in 51–100, 5 in 101–256), all high-candidate groups (median mean-candidates-per-task 17.1 against 6.5 for the others; the fit uses n only), worst 4.7 x the fit; none CAPPED. The list is `w3/over2x.json`. Kept: `w3/rows_all.json` (every group's replayed RTTs and run records, md5 `66e048ef…`), `w3/w3_analysis.json` (md5 `748b092e…`) and `w3/w3_analysis.md` (md5 `baea0426…`); `points.json` (md5 `cb510269…`) was scratch and is deleted with the shards, decodes and logs.
  - **What this changes.** Nothing registered. The pre-registered rule says the self-prefix arm is not dropped; building it remains the user's call and would add the controls in limit (1) first.

- 2026-10-15 — **W3 self-prefix pre-check: spec filed before any job runs** (S4; the user's "yes W3" relayed by the expert). **INTERIM-DESCRIPTIVE, MYOPIC-REPLAY (offline replayed regret on the group's own tasks at t = 0, not live latency); post-interim, no program alpha, diagnostic only: it gates nothing registered and relabels nothing.** Preceding looks: Arm 1b look 1, look 2, and the Arm 1b replay diagnostic (S5, 2026-10-15). Question: if the model's own decoded prefix is frozen, does the ILS completion beat the model's own completion by enough that training on self-decoded prefixes (Arm 2 K = 2 only reorders LABEL placements, so it never shows the model its own errors) is worth building? PASS builds nothing by itself; it only keeps the idea alive.
  - **Pins.** Branch `rp/uncut-w3` from reference-physics `d24f679c`. Decode, prep and replay are S5's `scripts_cosim/uncut_replay_diag.py` (identical to `e1a577fe`), unchanged; the engine is `scripts_cosim/uncut_ils.py`, byte-identical to `301f293f` (S5's pin) at `d24f679c` (`git diff 301f293f d24f679c -- scripts_cosim/uncut_ils.py` empty). `src/` at `d24f679c` differs from S5's `301f293f` only by the serve-fast files (`serve_fast.py`, `scheduler.py`, `feature_builder.py`, `reduced_features.py`), whose flags are off unless `SERVEFAST_*` is set (the sbatches unset that prefix too); that path is bit-identical with the flags off (`tests/test_serve_fast.py`). Checkpoints: Arm 1b `gnn_eng` seed 1 and 2 `-final.pt` (epoch 100, the served ones), md5s `f8b8be77761a020b1179f0853ae2f0a2` and `95f391d1a1638f456cd3e091f01f10c6`, from `experiments/uncut_arm1b_ckpt_md5.json` at commit `172268e6` (the file's md5 itself is `c38e163f9b996a6f32db5510c3b1dbd6`; the "172268e6" cited in the interim looks is that commit); the job re-checks both md5s against the file before decoding. Decode at 1 torch thread, CPU, exactly S5's `decode_group` (the served `masked_topo` decode, alpha inf, replica reuse, `declared_pruning_v1_uncapped`). The decode identity against the trainer's logged val metrics was established by S5 for these two checkpoints at 6 threads and passes; at 1 thread it differs on one group for each of them (see 2026-10-14 DECODE IDENTITY) and the 1-thread decode is the served one, so it is used here as S5 used it. It is not re-run on the 1,108 val groups.
  - **Groups.** Val only (model-based class): every val group in 51–100 (195: 104 ILS-labelled, 91 hand-fallback) and in 101–256 (62: 21 ILS-labelled, 41 hand-fallback), from `replay_diag_draw.json` (the list `w3_draw.json`, md5 `add931858c14ab3c43e0e6016c0a89c3`). No train group, no top-up group. Hand-fallback groups are those whose label is the hand plan (no ILS label); reported separately and pooled.
  - **Prefix points (fixed now).** For each checkpoint and group, the model's own free decode (S5's) commits tasks in DAG topological order (Kahn, lowest task id first: `topological_task_order`), so a prefix is the first t tasks of that order with the model's own placements; t = floor(0.25 n) and floor(0.5 n). No other points.
  - **Completion.** The Arm 1 ILS engine (`uncut_ils.ils` through `decision_pass`, arm `cd_ib0`, S's ib0 form) with the prefix frozen (the prefix tasks are added to the engine's `queued` set, which pins them to the single start key; nothing else in the engine is edited), start = the model's own full plan (checked equal to the engine's start, and the prefix checked unchanged in the result, FAIL LOUD otherwise), one seed (engine seed 0), budget = round(B0 x (n - t) / n) evaluations where B0 is the engine's default for that group (200 x n x mean candidates, measured by one 1-evaluation pass per group with the frozen set empty). Wall cap 3,600 s per run, safety only; a run it stops is CAPPED and reported.
  - **Metric.** gain = (R(model plan) - R(prefix + ILS completion)) / R(model plan), R the fidelity replay of the group's own tasks (`replay_rtts`, S5's). Positive = the completion beats the model's.
  - **PASS (build self-prefix training) requires both**, in 51–100 or in 101–256, over all points pooled over seeds and prefix points and over both label classes: (i) gain >= 5 % on >= 30 % of the points; (ii) that share exceeds the share of points with gain <= -5 % by >= 15 pp (the noise floor: ILS optimises S, not the replay, and S5 saw 16–34 % S-better-but-replay-worse on hand groups). Otherwise FAIL and the self-prefix arm is dropped. The rule is not tuned after the data.
  - **Also reported** (descriptive): per bucket x label class x seed x prefix point, the median and mean gain with 95 % group-bootstrap CIs (2,000 resamples of groups, a group's points kept together, fixed seed), the two shares with CIs, and ILS's own S-vs-replay disagreement on these runs (points where the engine's S says the completion is better and the replay says worse, and worse by >= 5 %). Integrity: the label plan is replayed in every group and must equal the stored RTT (relative 1e-9); decode-failed runs, CAPPED runs and runs whose plan equals the model's are counted.
  - **Cost.** CPU only, 1 thread per run. ILS wall is linear in budget (S5's job 874078 used its full budget on every run); S5's 724 runs of groups > 50 fit `search_wall = 0.0072059 x n^2.0689` s at full budget (about 1.5 ms per evaluation, fixed overhead under 1 s per run). Applied to the val n and the two budgets: 51–100 5.9 CPU-h, 101–256 11.1, decode and replay under 1: about 17–18 CPU-h in total; cap 20 (the expert, 2026-10-15). A run whose search wall exceeds twice the fit (fit x (n - t)/n) is flagged OVER2X in its row and the log; the expert asked to be told. The fit is a log-linear one and S5's 101–256 runs had max/median 3.3, so an isolated trip is expected; I report every trip and stop the array if trips are systematic or the projected total passes 20 CPU-h. Jobs: one prep + decode + points job (4 CPUs), a 16-task ILS array, one analysis job; queue stays <= 44. Scratch under `/share/nikola.lukic/uncut_groups/w3/` on datalab, deleted after the result is filed; the analysis json/md and the per-group rows are kept (rows copied to `docs/lineages/uncut_groups_s0_v1/w3/`). Files: `scripts_cosim/uncut_w3_prefix.py`, `scripts_cosim/datalab/uncut_w3_{prep,ils,analyze}.sbatch`.

(1) **ep_bv selection metric:** the epochs 13 / 11 / 21 / 5 (gnn s1 / gnn s2 / twin s1 / twin s2) were chosen on `val/regret_masked_topo` (each `.val.json` `checkpoint_metric`), which is sidecar-floored and tracks exact-plan hits. **OBJECTIVE (ep_bv -> ep100)** is confirmed only if, on the 300 train groups already replayed, both hold: free-decode per-task match rises >= 3 pp from ep_bv to ep100 and train replay regret vs hand does not improve (final minus ep_bv within its CI of 0, or worse). gnn s2: match +5.85 pp, regret change +44.1 pp [-12.4, +141.5] -> both hold, so OBJECTIVE stands as confirmed on train 300 (val: +3.31 pp, change -3.7 pp [-15.4, +8.7]). twin s2: val fired (+4.85 pp, change -19.4 pp [-43.1, +3.9]), but on train the match rose +6.43 pp and regret improved (-57.1 pp [-125.9, -17.9], CI excludes 0) -> relabelled **OBJECTIVE (selection-biased, unconfirmed)**, not "not fired". Disclosure: train is fit-biased in the opposite direction (ep100 has fit those groups longer, so train favours ep100 improving), so a confirmation on train is strong and a non-confirmation is ambiguous. gnn s1 and twin s1 stay not testable (val match +2.55 and +0.24 pp). There are no look-2 snapshots, so train is the only confirmation set. (2) **101-256 top-up scope:** the model-based classes EXPOSURE, OVERFIT/TOPO-SHIFT, UNDERFIT, AMBIGUOUS and OBJECTIVE were computed on the 62 val groups of 101-256 (21 ILS-labelled + 41 hand-fallback) and the 12 groups of that bucket in the 300-group train sample; none used the 19 top-up groups, which appear only in LABEL-SEARCH, so no label changes. Optional supplementary figures, not the ruled reading (the ruled val-only reading is all 62 val groups, because the top-up concern was train-group contamination, not label type): restricted to the 21 ILS-labelled val groups, EXPOSURE is computable from the stored cell (teacher-forced minus free-decode per-task match 25.1 / 25.2 / 26.7 / 31.8 pp for gnn s1 / gnn s2 / twin s1 / twin s2, still > 10 pp, still fires); UNDERFIT, OVERFIT/TOPO-SHIFT, AMBIGUOUS and OBJECTIVE are **not testable at n = 21 from existing outputs** (the train side is not stored per label type and the per-group replay output was deleted at cleanup, listed below). Their n = 62 figures stand as filed. **Power caveats that travel with the classes:** OBJECTIVE (gnn s2) fires under the filed rule, but the regret comparison is low-power (CI widths: val [-15.4, +8.7], train [-12.4, +141.5]); "not improved" is absence of evidence, not demonstrated flatness. OVERFIT/TOPO-SHIFT in 101-256 rests on 12 train groups and is quoted with n = 12 wherever it appears. **Deleted at cleanup (2026-10-15, after the result was filed), so these classes cannot be recomputed from files:** the graph shards (`shards/g_*.pkl`, the 1427 drawn groups' graphs), both decode directories (`decode/`, 1 thread, and `decode_t6/`, 6 threads: per-group decoded plans and teacher-forced hits for the 8 checkpoints), the per-group replay output (`replay/r_*.json`: every plan and replayed RTT per group) and the Slurm logs of the prep, probe, decode, replay, ILS and checks jobs. - 2026-10-14 — **Arm 1b replay diagnostic: why the plan copy rate tops out at 36 %. Spec filed before any output is read** (the user's request relayed by the expert; SCIENTIST's review adopted in full and replacing the expert's first classes; S5). **INTERIM-DESCRIPTIVE, diagnostic only:** not a gate, feeds no registered read, relabels nothing. Anything it motivates is post-interim, gets no program alpha and names this as a preceding look. No Arm 1/1b/2 item changes. K1(b) is untouched and never pooled with it (if the ILS gap on ILS groups differs materially from K1(b)'s, both are shown side by side with their state sets named). MYOPIC-REPLAY is printed on every table: the replay sees only the group's own tasks at t = 0, so it is offline regret, not live latency.
  - **Pins.** Branch `rp/uncut-replay-diag` from reference-physics `301f293f`. Replay engine: `scripts_cosim/uncut_ils.py` at `301f293f` (`decision_pass` with `job["dataset"]`, `_search_at_decision`, `ils`, `replay_rtts`); the sibling `8d7a6f8b` (rp/uncut-ils head) differs in `decision_pass`/labels and is not used. Model and decode code: `src/policy` and `src/placement` at `301f293f` are byte-identical to the Arm 1b pin `c94b9459` (`git diff c94b9459 301f293f` touches only `train_near_rtt.py`, the 301f293f logging change), so the checkpoints are decoded by the code they were trained with. Checkpoints: the 4 Arm 1b `-final.pt` (md5 file `experiments/uncut_arm1b_ckpt_md5.json`, 172268e6). Hand plan: cd_ib0's own `_pg_decide` plan (arm `cd_ib0`), as the engine returns it.
  - **Groups, drawn before any output was read** (`docs/lineages/uncut_groups_s0_v1/replay_diag_draw.json`, md5 f6af91dd69ff45539404137dbdd8a632, made by `scripts_cosim/uncut_replay_diag_draw.py` from the split artifact and the first line of each `placements.jsonl` only). Val: all 1108 heldout groups (split 613c8cbb). Train: 300 groups, `random.Random("uncut-replay-diag:v1:train")`. ILS subsample: `random.Random("uncut-replay-diag:v1:ils")`; 50 ILS-labelled val groups in each of 11-50, 51-100 and 101-256, all of them if fewer, and a bucket under 40 is topped up to 40 from the ILS-labelled train groups (a label property is tested, so a train group is valid; marked `topup_train`): 50 / 50 / 40 (101-256 has 21 val + 19 train). Hand-fallback matched subsample: 50 / 50 / 41 in the same buckets. Groups of <= 10 tasks carry exact labels (ILS-labelled: 12 val) and get no ILS. Val label types by bucket (exact / ILS / hand fallback): <=10 377 / 12 / 29; 11-50 0 / 197 / 236; 51-100 0 / 104 / 91; 101-256 0 / 21 / 41.
  - **Per group, at N = 0 on the same engine:** (1) the label (stored tied-optimal) plan; (2) each checkpoint's free `masked_topo` decode via the trainer's own closure (`make_partial_state_score_fn`, eval mode); (3) cd_ib0's plan; (4) one seeded uniformly random valid plan (`Random("uncut-replay-diag:v1:rand:<group>")`, per-task uniform over the group's candidate slate); (5) on the ILS and hand-fallback subsamples, 4 independent seeded ILS runs (seeds 0-3) from the label plan, each at the default budget (200 x n x mean candidates), each run's final best replayed, the best of the 4 scored.
  - **Identity checks (both void everything below on failure; stop and report).** IDENTITY: the label plan's replayed RTT equals the stored RTT at relative 1e-9, on every replayed group (the expert's first rule allowed 1 %; SCIENTIST's replaces it). DECODE IDENTITY: the free decode of each checkpoint on the 1108 val groups reproduces that run's logged final `val/mt_plan_exact` and `val/mt_task_acc_choice` exactly (W&B, last epoch).
  - **Metrics, always per bucket (<=10 / 11-50 / 51-100 / 101-256) AND per label type (exact <=10 / ILS r >= 0.10 / hand fallback), never pooled-only,** with group-bootstrap 95 % CIs (2000 resamples, seeded): `plan_exact` (the trainer's definition), per-task match on the free decode, replay regret (RTT(plan) - RTT(label)) / RTT(label) for model, hand and random; the model's regret split into exact-match and miss groups; H; share of groups where the model beats hand; share where hand beats the label; train-vs-val model regret; gnn vs twin; teacher-forced vs free-decode per-task accuracy.
  - **Classes (multi-label; every class that holds is reported with its numbers).**
    - LABEL-SEARCH (ILS-labelled groups only): the best of the 4 ILS runs beats the label by > 1 % replayed RTT on >= 20 % of the subsample. On hand-fallback groups the gap is reported on the matched subsample but does not fire (by design the label there is the hand plan).
    - BUG: the label is worse than hand by > 1e-9 on any ILS-labelled group.
    - S-MISMATCH: the label is worse than hand under replay on >= 10 % of the <= 10-task groups.
    - TOO-EASY: H = share_ILS x mean r (r = the label rule's stored (RTT_hand - RTT_ILS) / RTT_hand), task-weighted, on the ILS groups, both factors reported; fires when H < 1 % in both 51-100 and 101-256.
    - UNDERFIT: train free-decode regret - train hand regret >= 0 AND |train - val| < 0.5 pp.
    - OVERFIT / TOPO-SHIFT: val - train regret > 1 pp (CI excludes 0) AND train per-task accuracy - val > 5 pp.
    - SHIFT: D = (model - hand on live states) - (model - hand on corpus states), live states reweighted to the corpus bucket x opening (t < 360 s) mix, cluster bootstrap by cell; fires at D > 1 pp with CI excluding 0; hand-arm and learned-arm snapshots separately if both exist, a learned-arm-only set labelled "own-trajectory"; 200 live states replayed through the same engine. Needs the look-2 snapshot path (asked of S6); if none were kept, SHIFT is reported not testable.
    - OBJECTIVE: per-task accuracy rises from the early checkpoint to the final while val regret vs hand does not improve (the difference within its CI of 0). Only `-final.pt` and the best-val checkpoint (epochs 13 / 11 / 21 / 5 for gnn s1 / gnn s2 / twin s1 / twin s2) were saved, so the early point is the best-val checkpoint; epoch 1 does not exist and the test is labelled "early = best-val epoch".
    - AMBIGUOUS: >= 50 % of the non-copied plans in a bucket are within 0.5 % replay regret of the label.
    - EXPOSURE: teacher-forced per-task accuracy minus free-decode per-task match > 10 pp in the large buckets.
  - **Logistics.** Result JSON carries the full provenance (shas, md5s, seeds, host, cpu); no W&B (no training). Array on CPU-amd or rtx6000 CPUs, 1 torch thread per process, threads <= nproc, queue total <= 44. A timing probe runs first and the CPU-hour plan goes to the expert before anything over 20 CPU-h. The result is filed as one `INTERIM-DESCRIPTIVE` row in `docs/lineages/uncut_groups_s0_v1/interim_looks.md` (the look-2 snapshots are gate-state information) and as a dated descriptive entry here.

What is served at such a tie is the decoder's rule: the lowest (node_id, platform_id). So the served plan at a tie is a function of the rounding of the matmul that produced the logits, which is why an arithmetically equivalent but differently-shaped scorer changes plans, and why anything that changes the scorer's row set or width needs a decision-identity check, not only a logit tolerance.
